The dataset supports both English and Chinese and more importantly provides ground truth for both task completion and intention alignment, which allows for more fine-grained evaluation of mobile-use agents. A valuable resource for the community.
*   **Technical Approach:**  The IFRAgent framework is well-structured and leverages existing techniques (RAG, SOP extraction) in a novel way to solve the intention alignment problem. The separation into intention flow extraction and deployment phases makes the system modular and allows for plug-and-play capability.  The warm-up training for the query rewriter is a practical and effective way to improve personalization.
*   **Extensive Experiments:** The paper presents thorough experimental results across a wide range of mobile-use agents, including both open-source and closed-source models. The ablation studies, cross-dataset tests, and scale analysis provide evidence for the effectiveness, generalizability, and robustness of IFRAgent. The demonstration count analysis is interesting and provides some direction on how to improve the IFRAgent performance.
*   **Improved Metrics:** The authors specifically address the alignment level of their mobile-use agents with human intentions. While most existing benchmarks focus on task completion, this paper also uses the intention alignment rate metric (IAR), that requires the agent's action to exactly match the human-intent-aligned action to be counted as correct. This metric ensures a more fine-grained assessment of personalization.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Dependency on LLMs:** The IFRAgent framework heavily relies on large language models (LLMs) for its core components, which raises questions about the computational cost and scalability of the approach.  The paper does not discuss trade-offs between model size, performance, and computational requirements in detail.
*   **Limited User Study:** The dataset collection involved a limited number of users (9 users: 4 English and 5 Chinese speakers). This relatively small sample size could impact the generalizability of the results and might not capture the full diversity of human preferences and habits.
*   **Potential for Bias:**  Given the LLM foundation and limited user study, there is a potential for the system to learn and amplify biases present in the training data or the broader LLM knowledge base. This potential bias could lead to unintended consequences in terms of user experience or fairness.
*   **Clarity of Implementation Details:** While the paper outlines the overall architecture of IFRAgent, certain implementation details could be more clearly described. For example, details on the architectures and training methods used for the implicit and explicit intention flow agents are somewhat vague. It's unclear how these components interact to achieve the reported performance gains.
*   **Lack of Qualitative Analysis:** The paper primarily focuses on quantitative evaluation. A qualitative analysis of the generated personalized queries and SOPs would provide more insights into the IFRAgent's understanding of human intentions.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper demonstrates clear novelty in its problem formulation, dataset creation, and technical approach. Shifting the focus to personalize user intent and addressing it with the proposed framework significantly contributes to the current body of knowledge in mobile agent automation.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **8**.  The paper introduces a valuable problem, a novel dataset, and a functional framework. The experimental results validate the effectiveness of IFRAgent and its contributions to the field. However, the reliance on LLMs, the limited user study, and potential for bias represent challenges that need to be further addressed in future work. A score of 9 or 10 would require overcoming those limitations and a more complete understanding of how it functions.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### LLaMA-Based Models for Aspect-Based Sentiment Analysis
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08649v1)
- **Authors**: Jakub Šmíd, Pavel Přibáň, Pavel Král
- **Abstract**: While large language models (LLMs) show promise for various tasks, their performance in compound aspect-based sentiment analysis (ABSA) tasks lags behind fine-tuned models. However, the potential of LLMs fine-tuned for ABSA remains unexplored. This paper examines the capabilities of open-source LLMs fine-tuned for ABSA, focusing on LLaMA-based models. We evaluate the performance across four tasks and eight English datasets, finding that the fine-tuned Orca~2 model surpasses state-of-the-art results in all tasks. However, all models struggle in zero-shot and few-shot scenarios compared to fully fine-tuned ones. Additionally, we conduct error analysis to identify challenges faced by fine-tuned models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the use of fine-tuned, open-source LLaMA-based large language models (LLMs) for compound aspect-based sentiment analysis (ABSA).  It addresses the gap in research regarding the potential of fine-tuning these models for ABSA, comparing LLaMA and Orca 2 models across four tasks (ASTE, TASD, ACOS, ASQP) and eight datasets. The study evaluates performance in zero-shot, few-shot, and fully fine-tuned settings. A key finding is that the fine-tuned Orca 2 (13B) model achieves state-of-the-art results across all tasks.  The paper also includes an error analysis to identify common challenges for fine-tuned models, particularly concerning the prediction of opinion terms.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its systematic evaluation of open-source LLaMA-based models, specifically Orca 2, for the challenging task of compound ABSA.  While previous work has explored LLMs for ABSA, including using ChatGPT, and methods like QLoRA exist for efficient fine-tuning, this study fills the gap in demonstrating the specific effectiveness of *fine-tuned* open-source LLaMA variants, especially Orca 2, against prior SOTA methods. The error analysis is valuable as well, offering targeted insights. However, the methodology largely leverages existing techniques (fine-tuning, QLoRA), applying them to a new set of models and a specific task.

*   **Significance:** The study's significance stems from demonstrating that properly fine-tuned open-source LLMs can surpass previous, more task-specific architectures in ABSA. This is important because: 1) It democratizes access to high-performing ABSA models by leveraging open-source technology. 2) It offers a more efficient (parameter-wise) and potentially more generalizable solution compared to training task-specific models from scratch. 3) The identified challenges (opinion term prediction) can guide future research efforts in improving LLM-based ABSA.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper thoroughly evaluates models across multiple tasks and datasets, providing a robust assessment of performance.
    *   **Comparison with SOTA:** The clear comparison against previous state-of-the-art models provides valuable context for the results.
    *   **Error Analysis:** The error analysis offers insights into the limitations and potential areas for improvement.
    *   **Clear Methodology:** The experimental setup and fine-tuning procedures are clearly described, allowing for reproducibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Exploration of Prompting Techniques:** While the paper discusses the importance of prompting, there's limited exploration of different prompting strategies or prompt engineering techniques. It mainly focuses on a standard prompting method. Experimenting with alternative prompting strategies could potentially further improve zero/few-shot performance.
    *   **Generalizability of Findings:** The study focuses solely on English datasets. The performance of these fine-tuned models in other languages remains an open question.
    *   **Limited Error Analysis Depth:** While the error analysis is useful, delving deeper into *why* the models struggle with specific opinion terms or aspect categories could provide further insights.
    *   **Incremental Improvement:** While surpassing previous SOTA results is significant, the margin of improvement in some tasks is relatively small (under 1%).

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper's findings could influence the direction of ABSA research by encouraging the use of fine-tuned open-source LLMs. It could also motivate further exploration of prompting techniques and pre-training strategies to improve LLM performance in low-resource settings (zero-shot/few-shot ABSA). The identification of challenges related to opinion term prediction could drive targeted research efforts to address this specific issue.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **7**. While the paper doesn't introduce radically new techniques, it provides a valuable and thorough empirical study that demonstrates the effectiveness of fine-tuned open-source LLMs for ABSA. The systematic evaluation, clear methodology, and error analysis are strengths.  The limitations regarding limited prompting exploration and a small margin of improvement in some tasks detract slightly from the overall score. However, the paper makes a significant contribution by showing that Orca 2 can surpass other models in the field.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### UWB at WASSA-2024 Shared Task 2: Cross-lingual Emotion Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08650v1)
- **Authors**: Jakub Šmíd, Pavel Přibáň, Pavel Král
- **Abstract**: This paper presents our system built for the WASSA-2024 Cross-lingual Emotion Detection Shared Task. The task consists of two subtasks: first, to assess an emotion label from six possible classes for a given tweet in one of five languages, and second, to predict words triggering the detected emotions in binary and numerical formats. Our proposed approach revolves around fine-tuning quantized large language models, specifically Orca~2, with low-rank adapters (LoRA) and multilingual Transformer-based models, such as XLM-R and mT5. We enhance performance through machine translation for both subtasks and trigger word switching for the second subtask. The system achieves excellent performance, ranking 1st in numerical trigger words detection, 3rd in binary trigger words detection, and 7th in emotion detection.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper describes the University of West Bohemia's (UWB) system for the WASSA-2024 Cross-lingual Emotion Detection Shared Task. The system addresses two subtasks: emotion classification from a given tweet in five languages and identifying trigger words associated with the detected emotions, both in binary and numerical formats. The proposed approach uses fine-tuning of quantized large language models (LLMs), specifically Orca 2, with low-rank adapters (LoRA), alongside multilingual Transformer-based models like XLM-R and mT5. The system enhances performance using machine translation to expand the training data and trigger word switching for the second subtask. The system achieved 1st place in numerical trigger word detection, 3rd in binary trigger word detection, and 7th in emotion detection.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**  The paper's novelty lies primarily in its combination of existing techniques rather than introducing fundamentally new methods.  Fine-tuning pre-trained models (Orca 2, XLM-R, mT5) is a common practice in NLP.  The use of LoRA for parameter-efficient fine-tuning of large language models is also well-established.  Machine translation for cross-lingual transfer and data augmentation is a standard technique. Trigger word switching appears to be a less commonly used but not necessarily novel technique. The specific combination of these methods, especially using QLoRA on Orca 2 for the emotion classification task, might represent a minor incremental improvement in applying known methods to this specific task.

**Significance:** The significance of the paper is moderate. Achieving top ranks in a shared task demonstrates the effectiveness of the chosen approach for *this specific dataset and task*.  However, the results don't necessarily generalize to other emotion detection datasets or settings. The study validates the utility of fine-tuning quantized LLMs and machine translation for cross-lingual emotion detection, adding to the body of evidence supporting these techniques. A significant limitation of the work is its reliance on machine translation, which can introduce errors and biases. The study does not appear to provide significant new theoretical insights into cross-lingual emotion detection.

**Strengths:**

*   **Strong Performance:**  The system achieves competitive results in the WASSA-2024 shared task, indicating a well-engineered system.
*   **Clear Methodology:** The paper clearly describes the system architecture, training procedures, and evaluation metrics.
*   **Ablation Analysis (Implied):** While not explicitly stated as an ablation, the comparisons between using only the original English dataset and incorporating translated data suggest an attempt to understand the contribution of each component.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Novelty:**  The paper doesn't introduce groundbreaking techniques but rather combines existing methods.
*   **Limited Generalizability:** The specific dataset and task might not generalize well to other scenarios.
*   **Dependence on Machine Translation:** Reliance on machine translation introduces a potential source of error and limits the robustness of the system.
*   **Lack of Deep Analysis:** While the results are reported, a deeper analysis of *why* certain techniques work better in specific scenarios is lacking. The "Discussion" section is somewhat superficial. For example, the authors suggest trigger word detection tasks are "more prone to translation errors" - but provide no data or further analysis to support this claim.
*   **Limited Exploration of Trigger Word Switching:** While the trigger word switching technique is mentioned, its impact and effectiveness could be explored further.
*   **Limited Scope of Evaluation:** The evaluation is primarily focused on the shared task data and doesn't include a broader assessment of the system's performance on other datasets or in different languages.

**Potential Influence:** The paper can serve as a useful reference for researchers working on cross-lingual emotion detection, demonstrating a practical approach to combine existing techniques. It provides empirical evidence supporting the effectiveness of specific models (Orca 2, XLM-R, mT5) and fine-tuning strategies for this task. However, its impact on the field is likely to be incremental rather than transformative.

**Score: 6**

**Rationale:** The paper demonstrates a well-executed application of known techniques to a specific shared task, resulting in competitive performance. It showcases a practical approach to cross-lingual emotion detection but lacks significant novelty or deep insights. The main strength is its empirical validation of the chosen methods, but the lack of more detailed analysis and broader evaluation limits its overall impact. The incremental nature of the contribution and reliance on machine translation justify a score in the moderate range.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Prompt-and-Check: Using Large Language Models to Evaluate Communication Protocol Compliance in Simulation-Based Training
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08652v1)
- **Authors**: Vishakha Lall, Yisi Liu
- **Abstract**: Accurate evaluation of procedural communication compliance is essential in simulation-based training, particularly in safety-critical domains where adherence to compliance checklists reflects operational competence. This paper explores a lightweight, deployable approach using prompt-based inference with open-source large language models (LLMs) that can run efficiently on consumer-grade GPUs. We present Prompt-and-Check, a method that uses context-rich prompts to evaluate whether each checklist item in a protocol has been fulfilled, solely based on transcribed verbal exchanges. We perform a case study in the maritime domain with participants performing an identical simulation task, and experiment with models such as LLama 2 7B, LLaMA 3 8B and Mistral 7B, running locally on an RTX 4070 GPU. For each checklist item, a prompt incorporating relevant transcript excerpts is fed into the model, which outputs a compliance judgment. We assess model outputs against expert-annotated ground truth using classification accuracy and agreement scores. Our findings demonstrate that prompting enables effective context-aware reasoning without task-specific training. This study highlights the practical utility of LLMs in augmenting debriefing, performance feedback, and automated assessment in training environments.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Prompt-and-Check," a lightweight method for evaluating communication protocol compliance in simulation-based training using open-source large language models (LLMs). The method leverages prompt engineering to assess whether specific checklist items in a protocol have been fulfilled based on transcribed verbal exchanges. The authors demonstrate their approach in the maritime domain, using a simulation dataset where deck officers respond to emergency navigational events. They evaluate LLaMA 2 7B, LLaMA 3 8B, and Mistral 7B models, showing that prompt-based inference enables context-aware reasoning without task-specific fine-tuning. The study emphasizes the potential of LLMs to augment debriefing, performance feedback, and automated assessment in training environments. Key elements include a two-stage context selection method (temporal and semantic) to refine the input for the LLMs, and schema-constrained parsing to improve output reliability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper demonstrates the application of LLMs for a specific task – evaluating compliance in simulated training environments from natural language transcripts. While the use of LLMs for various tasks is not entirely new, the focus on noisy, multi-turn dialogue transcripts and the combination of temporal and semantic context selection adds a layer of originality. The schema-constrained parsing to enforce valid JSON output is another potentially valuable contribution.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Practical Application:** The paper tackles a real-world problem of assessing communication compliance, which is relevant to many high-stakes domains.
    *   **Lightweight & Deployable:**  The use of open-source LLMs that can run efficiently on consumer-grade GPUs makes the approach practical and deployable, especially in resource-constrained settings.
    *   **Clear Methodology:** The paper clearly outlines the Prompt-and-Check methodology, including context selection, prompt design, and output validation.
    *   **Ablation Study:** The ablation study provides valuable insights into the effectiveness of different context selection strategies.
    *   **Schema Constrained Parsing:**  This greatly limits the possibility of hallucinations and invalid responses.
    *   **Focus on Interpretability:** The generation of justifications alongside compliance judgments enhances the interpretability and trustworthiness of the assessment.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Dataset:** While the maritime simulation dataset is appropriate for the case study, its size and specific nature may limit the generalizability of the findings. Expanding the dataset to include other domains would strengthen the conclusions.
    *   **Modest Performance Gains:** While LLaMA 3 8B achieves the best results, the performance difference between the models is not dramatically significant. It is possible other models may yield better performance.
    *   **Reliance on Expert-Annotated Labels:** The evaluation relies on expert-annotated ground truth labels. Inter-annotator agreement is not reported, which could affect the reliability of the evaluation.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Prompt Engineering Techniques:** While the prompt design is described, the paper does not explore the sensitivity of the results to different prompt formats or content. A more extensive exploration of prompt engineering techniques could further optimize performance.
    *   **Lack of statistical significance analysis:** It is not entirely clear if the reported performance differences across LLMs are statistically significant.

**Significance:**

The paper has the potential to be significant because it offers a practical, accessible, and interpretable method for automating compliance assessment. This could have a significant impact on training and safety audits in various high-stakes domains.

However, the limitations mentioned above temper the overall significance. The potential impact on the field relies on further generalization and validation of the approach across different datasets and more in-depth exploration of prompting strategies. More detailed statistical significance analysis of the reported findings would also be valuable.

**Score: 6**

**Rationale:**

A score of 6 reflects the paper's moderate novelty and potential significance. The paper demonstrates a practical application of LLMs for a real-world problem with a clearly defined methodology and interesting results. However, the limitations regarding dataset size, the lack of statistical significance analysis, and the scope for further exploration of prompt engineering mean that the paper's overall contribution is somewhat limited. While promising, more work is needed to establish the generalizability and robustness of the approach and increase its overall impact.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### LLM driven Text-to-Table Generation through Sub-Tasks Guidance and Iterative Refinement
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08653v1)
- **Authors**: Rajmohan C, Sarthak Harne, Arvind Agarwal
- **Abstract**: Transforming unstructured text into structured data is a complex task, requiring semantic understanding, reasoning, and structural comprehension. While Large Language Models (LLMs) offer potential, they often struggle with handling ambiguous or domain-specific data, maintaining table structure, managing long inputs, and addressing numerical reasoning. This paper proposes an efficient system for LLM-driven text-to-table generation that leverages novel prompting techniques. Specifically, the system incorporates two key strategies: breaking down the text-to-table task into manageable, guided sub-tasks and refining the generated tables through iterative self-feedback. We show that this custom task decomposition allows the model to address the problem in a stepwise manner and improves the quality of the generated table. Furthermore, we discuss the benefits and potential risks associated with iterative self-feedback on the generated tables while highlighting the trade-offs between enhanced performance and computational cost. Our methods achieve strong results compared to baselines on two complex text-to-table generation datasets available in the public domain.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and rigorous evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper introduces a system for text-to-table generation using large language models (LLMs).  The core innovation lies in its prompting strategy, which involves:

1.  **Intermediate Sub-tasks Guidance:** Breaking down the complex task into smaller, guided sub-tasks like header explanation, abbreviation expansion, data format resolution, entity extraction/grouping, and finally, table generation.

2.  **Iterative Self-Refinement:**  Using the LLM to evaluate its own generated table based on criteria like data completeness, data relevance, table structure consistency, and domain-specific feedback, and then regenerating the table. This refinement is explored at different levels of granularity (table, row, and cell).

The authors compare their approach against standard prompting baselines (Zero-shot, Few-shot, Chain-of-Thought) on the RotoWire and LiveSum datasets.  The results indicate that the proposed system achieves strong performance, particularly when incorporating intermediate sub-task guidance and cell-level self-feedback.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the combination of intermediate sub-task guidance with iterative self-refinement for text-to-table generation.  While both task decomposition and self-feedback have been explored separately in other contexts, applying them *specifically in this manner* to the text-to-table problem is a key differentiator. The investigation of different granularities for the self-feedback loop (table, row, cell) adds to the novelty.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant challenge in leveraging LLMs for structured data generation. Text-to-table generation is an important task with applications in data integration, knowledge graph construction, and summarization. Demonstrating an effective prompting strategy that improves performance without requiring resource-intensive fine-tuning is valuable. The findings regarding the effectiveness of cell-level feedback compared to table-level feedback provide useful insights for future research and system design.  The state-of-the-art performance on well-established datasets highlights the potential of the proposed approach.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly defines the challenges of text-to-table generation using LLMs.
    *   **Well-Defined Methodology:** The proposed system is well-described, with clear explanations of the sub-tasks and refinement process.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The paper includes experiments on two standard datasets and compares against relevant baselines and prior art.
    *   **Insightful Analysis:**  The analysis of the results, including the discussion of the trade-offs between different feedback granularities and computational cost, is valuable.
    *   **Practical Relevance:** The use of prompting strategies rather than fine-tuning makes the approach more practical for real-world applications.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Model Exploration:** While the paper uses Llama-3-70B-Instruct as the primary model, it would be strengthened by evaluating the approach on a wider range of LLMs.
    *   **Dataset Dependency:** Although two datasets were used, both are from the sports domain. Assessing the generalizability to other domains (e.g., finance, medicine) would be beneficial.
    *   **Hyperparameter Tuning Details:** The details about the hyperparameter tuning (e.g., number of iterations, specific prompting phrasing) are somewhat limited. Providing more information would enhance reproducibility.
    *   **Cost Analysis:** While the paper mentions the cost implications of iterative self-refinement, a more detailed cost analysis (e.g., API token usage) would be valuable.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper's approach offers a promising way to improve the performance of LLMs for text-to-table generation without the need for extensive fine-tuning. It could influence future research in this area by highlighting the benefits of task decomposition and iterative refinement. The insights into feedback granularity could guide the development of more effective self-improvement strategies for LLMs. The system also addresses the challenges associated with complex or domain-specific unstructured data.

*   **Score Rationale:** The paper presents a novel combination of techniques with strong empirical results. While there are some limitations regarding model exploration and dataset dependency, the clarity of the methodology, the insightful analysis, and the potential impact on the field justify a high score. However, it stops short of a truly exceptional breakthrough that would warrant a higher rating.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### $\text{M}^{2}$LLM: Multi-view Molecular Representation Learning with Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08657v1)
- **Authors**: Jiaxin Ju, Yizhen Zheng, Huan Yee Koh, Can Wang, Shirui Pan
- **Abstract**: Accurate molecular property prediction is a critical challenge with wide-ranging applications in chemistry, materials science, and drug discovery. Molecular representation methods, including fingerprints and graph neural networks (GNNs), achieve state-of-the-art results by effectively deriving features from molecular structures. However, these methods often overlook decades of accumulated semantic and contextual knowledge. Recent advancements in large language models (LLMs) demonstrate remarkable reasoning abilities and prior knowledge across scientific domains, leading us to hypothesize that LLMs can generate rich molecular representations when guided to reason in multiple perspectives. To address these gaps, we propose $\text{M}^{2}$LLM, a multi-view framework that integrates three perspectives: the molecular structure view, the molecular task view, and the molecular rules view. These views are fused dynamically to adapt to task requirements, and experiments demonstrate that $\text{M}^{2}$LLM achieves state-of-the-art performance on multiple benchmarks across classification and regression tasks. Moreover, we demonstrate that representation derived from LLM achieves exceptional performance by leveraging two core functionalities: the generation of molecular embeddings through their encoding capabilities and the curation of molecular features through advanced reasoning processes.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces M²LLM, a novel multi-view molecular representation learning framework. M²LLM integrates three perspectives of molecular data: the molecular structure view (encoded from SMILES strings using LLMs), the molecular task view (contextualizing molecules within specific prediction tasks), and the molecular rules view (deriving interpretable features based on scientific knowledge and data patterns using LLMs). These views are dynamically fused to create a unified representation used for downstream prediction tasks.  The authors demonstrate state-of-the-art performance on multiple molecular property prediction benchmarks and highlight the potential of LLMs for molecular representation learning through encoding and reasoning capabilities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The core novelty lies in the multi-view approach and the integration of LLMs in all three views. While previous works have explored LLMs for molecular representation, they primarily focused on using SMILES strings directly or combining LLMs with GNNs. The explicit separation and fusion of structural, task-specific, and rule-based views, all leveraged through LLMs, is a significant advance. The molecular feature curation through the use of LLMs for knowledge gathering and reasoning is a good idea.

*   **Significance:**  Achieving state-of-the-art performance on multiple benchmarks across both classification and regression tasks demonstrates the practical significance of the proposed approach. The paper also identifies the dual capabilities of LLMs for molecular embedding generation and molecular feature curation, addressing a crucial gap in previous research. The results demonstrate better performance compared to various baselines including RF+ECFP4, GNN-based and Transformer-based models. Furthermore, they conduct an ablation study to analyze the contribution of each view, highlighting that the molecular structure view is more significant for classification, whereas molecular rule and task views are more useful for regression tasks. These results enhance the credibility of using LLM-based approaches for molecular property prediction tasks.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The multi-view approach is well-motivated and leverages different aspects of molecular information effectively.
    *   The integration of LLMs is comprehensive, utilizing their encoding and reasoning capabilities.
    *   The experiments are thorough, covering multiple datasets and tasks.
    *   Ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each view.
    *   The ablation studies give insights into what tasks different components of LLMs are most useful for.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Although the results are strong, the ablation could have benefited from more detailed discussion on failure cases.
    *   The reliance on LLMs makes the approach computationally expensive, and the paper does not thoroughly explore the cost implications.
    *   The rule-to-feature code translator, while conceptually useful, is not explored in detail; more information should be provided about its implementation.

*   **Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of molecular representation learning. The multi-view approach, combined with the comprehensive utilization of LLMs, addresses limitations in previous methods and achieves state-of-the-art results. While the computational cost and certain implementation details could be further explored, the paper's strengths outweigh its weaknesses. The paper provides insights into the role of LLMs in molecular learning and provides a strong baseline for future work in this direction.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### Aryabhata: An exam-focused language model for JEE Math
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08665v1)
- **Authors**: Ritvik Rastogi, Sachin Dharashivkar, Sandeep Varma
- **Abstract**: We present Aryabhata 1.0, a compact 7B parameter math reasoning model optimized for the Indian academic exam, the Joint Entrance Examination (JEE). Despite rapid progress in large language models (LLMs), current models often remain unsuitable for educational use. Aryabhata 1.0 is built by merging strong open-weight reasoning models, followed by supervised fine-tuning (SFT) with curriculum learning on verified chain-of-thought (CoT) traces curated through best-of-$n$ rejection sampling. To further boost performance, we apply reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) using A2C objective with group-relative advantage estimation along with novel exploration strategies such as Adaptive Group Resizing and Temperature Scaling. Evaluated on both in-distribution (JEE Main 2025) and out-of-distribution (MATH, GSM8K) benchmarks, Aryabhata outperforms existing models in accuracy and efficiency, while offering pedagogically useful step-by-step reasoning. We release Aryabhata as a foundation model to advance exam-centric, open-source small language models. This marks our first open release for community feedback (https://huggingface.co/PhysicsWallahAI/Aryabhata-1.0); PW is actively training future models to further improve learning outcomes for students.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Aryabhata 1.0, a 7B parameter language model designed specifically for math reasoning in the context of the Indian Joint Entrance Examination (JEE). It addresses the limitations of existing LLMs in the education space, which often lack accuracy, transparency, and efficiency. Aryabhata 1.0 is built through model merging (combining Qwen, AceMath, and DeepSeek distillations), supervised fine-tuning (SFT) with curriculum learning on verified chain-of-thought (CoT) traces, and reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR). The model is evaluated on in-distribution (JEE Main 2025) and out-of-distribution (MATH, GSM8K) benchmarks, demonstrating improvements in accuracy, efficiency, and pedagogical usability compared to other models. The authors make Aryabhata 1.0 available as a foundation model.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Targeted Problem and Practical Motivation:** The paper directly tackles a real-world problem: improving math education in the context of a specific, high-stakes exam (JEE). The focus on accuracy, transparency, and efficiency aligns well with the needs of students and educators.
*   **Model Merging Approach:** The use of model merging to combine different strengths from existing models (math fluency, reasoning accuracy, long CoT traces) is a reasonable and potentially effective strategy.
*   **Careful Training Methodology:** The combination of SFT with curriculum learning and RLVR demonstrates a well-thought-out training process. The use of verifiable rewards is particularly beneficial in mathematical reasoning, where correctness can be objectively assessed. The innovative exploration strategies like Adaptive Group Resizing and Temperature Scaling in RLVR contribute to its effectiveness.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The model is evaluated on multiple benchmarks, including both in-distribution and out-of-distribution datasets. This provides a good assessment of the model's performance and generalization capabilities.
*   **Open Release:** The availability of Aryabhata 1.0 as a foundation model encourages further research and development in exam-centric, open-source small language models.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Technical Depth:** While the paper describes the methodology, it lacks in-depth details on certain key aspects. For example, the exact weighting scheme for model merging (how α, β, and γ are chosen) could be explained more thoroughly. Specific details on the prompts used for different stages (question cleaning, answer matching, LLM judgment in evaluation) are relegated to the appendix and could have been presented or summarized more prominently.
*   **Proprietary Dataset:** The reliance on a proprietary dataset from Physics Wallah is a significant limitation. This makes it difficult for other researchers to reproduce the results and prevents a completely open comparison with other models. The rationale that the data is core intellectual property limits external validation.
*   **Evaluation Metric Limitations:** While Pass@1 is common, it doesn't fully capture the nuance of pedagogical usefulness, such as clarity of reasoning or ease of understanding. More qualitative analysis or user studies could have been included.
*   **Lack of Novelty in Core Techniques:** The individual techniques (model merging, SFT, RLVR) are not inherently novel. The novelty lies in the specific application, combination, and tuning of these techniques for the targeted problem. While it does have innovations for exploration strategies in RLVR, the argument for novelty is weaker.

**Significance and Impact:**

The paper's significance lies in its practical focus on improving math education using LLMs. By targeting a specific exam (JEE) and addressing the needs of Indian students, the work has the potential to have a real-world impact. The open release of Aryabhata 1.0 could also stimulate further research in this area.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper demonstrates a good combination of existing techniques to address a specific and important problem. While the individual techniques are not groundbreaking, the careful tuning and application of these techniques, particularly the integration of RLVR with specialized exploration methods, show thoughtful engineering and a focus on practical usability. However, the limitations associated with the proprietary dataset and lack of novelty in core techniques, alongside the limited details on implementation, lower the score.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### Learning Generalizable and Efficient Image Watermarking via Hierarchical Two-Stage Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08667v1)
- **Authors**: Ke Liu, Xuanhan Wang, Qilong Zhang, Lianli Gao, Jingkuan Song
- **Abstract**: Deep image watermarking, which refers to enable imperceptible watermark embedding and reliable extraction in cover images, has shown to be effective for copyright protection of image assets. However, existing methods face limitations in simultaneously satisfying three essential criteria for generalizable watermarking: 1) invisibility (imperceptible hide of watermarks), 2) robustness (reliable watermark recovery under diverse conditions), and 3) broad applicability (low latency in watermarking process). To address these limitations, we propose a Hierarchical Watermark Learning (HiWL), a two-stage optimization that enable a watermarking model to simultaneously achieve three criteria. In the first stage, distribution alignment learning is designed to establish a common latent space with two constraints: 1) visual consistency between watermarked and non-watermarked images, and 2) information invariance across watermark latent representations. In this way, multi-modal inputs including watermark message (binary codes) and cover images (RGB pixels) can be well represented, ensuring the invisibility of watermarks and robustness in watermarking process thereby. The second stage employs generalized watermark representation learning to establish a disentanglement policy for separating watermarks from image content in RGB space. In particular, it strongly penalizes substantial fluctuations in separated RGB watermarks corresponding to identical messages. Consequently, HiWL effectively learns generalizable latent-space watermark representations while maintaining broad applicability. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of proposed method. In particular, it achieves 7.6\% higher accuracy in watermark extraction than existing methods, while maintaining extremely low latency (100K images processed in 8s).
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Hierarchical Watermark Learning (HiWL), a novel two-stage optimization framework for deep image watermarking.  HiWL aims to simultaneously achieve invisibility, robustness, and broad applicability (low latency), which the authors argue are often conflicting goals in existing watermarking methods.  The first stage, distribution alignment, establishes a common latent space using visual consistency and information invariance constraints.  This stage facilitates robust representation of watermarks alongside cover images.  The second stage, generalized watermark representation learning, disentangles watermarks from image content in RGB space by penalizing fluctuations in RGB watermarks corresponding to identical messages.  The authors demonstrate through experiments that HiWL outperforms existing methods in terms of watermark extraction accuracy, while maintaining low latency, indicating improved generalizability and efficiency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the proposed two-stage optimization strategy, designed specifically to address the trade-offs between invisibility, robustness, and broad applicability in deep image watermarking.  The combination of distribution alignment in the latent space, followed by generalized RGB representation learning, is a unique approach.  While latent space and RGB-based watermarking techniques are individually explored in prior work, the *hierarchical* combination and the specific loss functions designed for each stage appear novel. The single-shot paradigm to satisfy broad applicability is significant.
* **Significance:** If the claims hold up under further scrutiny, the paper could significantly impact the field. The development of a single-shot deep watermarking method capable of achieving a good balance across all three of invisibility, robustness, and broad applicability will be valuable to AIGC communities. The detailed ablation studies and comparisons against existing approaches is also a strong point.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing deep watermarking methods.
    * **Well-Defined Approach:** The proposed HiWL framework is well-defined, with a clear explanation of each stage and the rationale behind the design choices.
    * **Comprehensive Experiments:** The experimental evaluation is extensive, including comparisons against various state-of-the-art methods, ablation studies, and tests under different noise conditions. The use of multiple datasets (including generated images) is appreciated. The discussion of a RGB residual attack is significant.
    * **Performance Gains:** HiWL demonstrates significant performance improvements over existing methods.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Complexity:** While the two-stage optimization is well-motivated, it also adds complexity to the model. The framework has multiple hyperparameters that can require significant tuning to get good results.
    * **Limited Attack Scope:**  The attacks considered (while substantial), may not be fully representative of all real-world scenarios.  More targeted adversarial attacks specific to the architecture might reveal further vulnerabilities.
    * **Generality of Generalization:** While the method shows improved generalization across datasets, the true test of generalization would be to assess the model's performance on completely unseen and significantly different image distributions.
    * **Potential Reliance on Training Data:** Deep learning methods are fundamentally limited by the data on which they are trained. The reliance on MS-COCO, ImageNet, etc., may not translate well to specialized data (e.g., medical imagery, satellite images).

* **Potential Influence:**  The framework's performance, if replicable, could encourage further research in efficient and generalizable deep watermarking methods. Other researchers might build upon the proposed two-stage optimization strategy or explore alternative loss functions to further improve performance.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **8**.  The paper addresses an important problem in the field, presents a novel and well-defined approach, and demonstrates significant performance improvements over existing methods. The experimental evaluation is reasonably comprehensive, but some of my concerns are the model complexity, reliance on a few large datasets, and scope of tested attacks, preventing a higher score. The significance of the broad applicability via a single-shot paradigm is a high-impact result.  A few weaknesses exist regarding the potential reliance on training dataset characteristics, and the lack of completely novel attacks, the overall contribution is significant and should prompt further research.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### In-Context Learning as Nonparametric Conditional Probability Estimation: Risk Bounds and Optimality
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08673v1)
- **Authors**: Chenrui Liu, Falong Tan, Chuanlong Xie, Yicheng Zeng, Lixing Zhu
- **Abstract**: This paper investigates the expected excess risk of In-Context Learning (ICL) for multiclass classification. We model each task as a sequence of labeled prompt samples and a query input, where a pre-trained model estimates the conditional class probabilities of the query. The expected excess risk is defined as the average truncated Kullback-Leibler (KL) divergence between the predicted and ground-truth conditional class distributions, averaged over a specified family of tasks. We establish a new oracle inequality for the expected excess risk based on KL divergence in multiclass classification. This allows us to derive tight upper and lower bounds for the expected excess risk in transformer-based models, demonstrating that the ICL estimator achieves the minimax optimal rate - up to a logarithmic factor - for conditional probability estimation. From a technical standpoint, our results introduce a novel method for controlling generalization error using the uniform empirical covering entropy of the log-likelihood function class. Furthermore, we show that multilayer perceptrons (MLPs) can also perform ICL and achieve this optimal rate under specific assumptions, suggesting that transformers may not be the exclusive architecture capable of effective ICL.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper provides a theoretical analysis of in-context learning (ICL) for multi-class classification. The authors model ICL as a nonparametric conditional probability estimation problem. Their main contribution is deriving an oracle inequality bounding the expected excess risk (measured using a truncated KL divergence) in terms of approximation, optimization, and generalization errors. The generalization error is controlled using the uniform empirical covering entropy of the log-likelihood function class. They establish minimax-optimal rates for transformer-based ICL, showing that ICL achieves the optimal rate (up to log factors) for conditional probability estimation. Furthermore, they demonstrate that multilayer perceptrons (MLPs) can also achieve this optimal rate under certain conditions, suggesting that transformers are not the only architectures capable of effective ICL.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Relaxing Assumptions:** The paper takes a step toward bridging the gap between theory and practice by relaxing commonly made assumptions in previous ICL analyses. For example, it considers multi-class classification tasks, analyzes transformers with multi-head dot-product attention, and focuses on the task-scaling regime (large number of pretraining tasks with fixed context length), which aligns better with real-world scenarios. This is a significant improvement over much of the existing theoretical literature.
    *   **New Oracle Inequality:** The introduction of a new oracle inequality for the expected excess risk based on KL divergence in multi-class classification is novel. The decomposition into approximation, optimization, and generalization errors is a standard and valuable approach for analyzing learning algorithms. The use of the uniform empirical covering entropy to control generalization is a technically interesting contribution.
    *   **MLP Result:** The result demonstrating that MLPs can achieve optimal ICL rates challenges the transformer-centric view of ICL. This finding is significant because it opens avenues for exploring simpler and potentially more efficient architectures for ICL.
    *   **Theoretical Rigor:** The paper presents a mathematically rigorous analysis, with detailed proofs provided in the appendices.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Truncated KL Divergence:** While using a truncated KL divergence to address the unboundedness issue is a standard trick, it introduces an artificial element into the analysis. A truly satisfying theory would ideally avoid this truncation and handle the full KL divergence directly or propose other robust risk measures.
    *   **Assumptions on Probability Distributions:** The assumption that estimated probabilities are bounded away from zero (Assumption 2) is strong and potentially restrictive. While it is necessary for controlling the complexity of the log-likelihood function class, it limits the applicability of the results.
    *   **Model Class Restrictions:** Even though it extends previous work, the model class is still a simplification of real-world transformers.
    *   **Focus on Task Scaling:** While focusing on the task-scaling regime is more practical than considering asymptotic context scaling, it still does not fully capture the challenges of few-shot scenarios where both task diversity and context length are limited.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    The paper's novelty lies in: 1) the relaxed assumptions (multiclass, MHAs) 2) the new oracle inequality, and 3) the MLP result which provides insights beyond the standard transformer paradigm. These provide meaningful contributions to the theoretical understanding of ICL.

*   **Potential Impact:**

    The paper has the potential to influence the field by encouraging research on more practical theoretical models of ICL and by motivating the exploration of alternative architectures for ICL. The technical tools developed in the paper (e.g., the covering entropy analysis) may be useful in other related areas.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**

The paper makes a significant contribution by moving towards more realistic ICL models and providing a new theoretical framework for analysis. The findings regarding the capabilities of MLPs in ICL are important and timely. However, the reliance on the truncated KL divergence, distributional assumptions and still simplified model class weaken the overall impact. While technically sound and innovative in certain aspects, the limitations prevent it from being a truly groundbreaking contribution. The work provides interesting insights and could serve as an inspiration to other researchers seeking more comprehensive theoretical analyses of ICL, thus justifying the score of 7.

- **Classification**: stat.ML
- **Score**: 7/10

### MMIF-AMIN: Adaptive Loss-Driven Multi-Scale Invertible Dense Network for Multimodal Medical Image Fusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08679v1)
- **Authors**: Tao Luo, Weihua Xu
- **Abstract**: Multimodal medical image fusion (MMIF) aims to integrate images from different modalities to produce a comprehensive image that enhances medical diagnosis by accurately depicting organ structures, tissue textures, and metabolic information. Capturing both the unique and complementary information across multiple modalities simultaneously is a key research challenge in MMIF. To address this challenge, this paper proposes a novel image fusion method, MMIF-AMIN, which features a new architecture that can effectively extract these unique and complementary features. Specifically, an Invertible Dense Network (IDN) is employed for lossless feature extraction from individual modalities. To extract complementary information between modalities, a Multi-scale Complementary Feature Extraction Module (MCFEM) is designed, which incorporates a hybrid attention mechanism, convolutional layers of varying sizes, and Transformers. An adaptive loss function is introduced to guide model learning, addressing the limitations of traditional manually-designed loss functions and enhancing the depth of data mining. Extensive experiments demonstrate that MMIF-AMIN outperforms nine state-of-the-art MMIF methods, delivering superior results in both quantitative and qualitative analyses. Ablation experiments confirm the effectiveness of each component of the proposed method. Additionally, extending MMIF-AMIN to other image fusion tasks also achieves promising performance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MMIF-AMIN, a novel deep learning architecture for multimodal medical image fusion (MMIF). The architecture leverages an Invertible Dense Network (IDN) for lossless feature extraction from individual modalities (MRI and SPECT), a Multi-scale Complementary Feature Extraction Module (MCFEM) to capture complementary information across modalities using a hybrid attention mechanism (CBAM), varying size convolutional layers, and Transformers, and an adaptive loss function to guide the learning process by relying on source image data features instead of manually setting parameters. The authors claim MMIF-AMIN outperforms existing state-of-the-art MMIF methods in both quantitative and qualitative analyses. Ablation studies are included to demonstrate the effectiveness of each module. Further, the method is tested on MRI-PET and PC-GFP fusion task showcasing its generalization abilities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents several novel aspects:
    *   The use of IDN for lossless feature extraction in each modality is a good design choice. While INNs have been used in MMIF before, the specific implementation and integration here is novel.
    *   The MCFEM, combining CBAM, varying kernel size convolutions, and Transformers, offers a flexible approach to capturing both local and global context within and across modalities.
    *   The adaptive loss function, driven by image characteristics rather than manual tuning, is a potentially significant contribution. It addresses a known limitation of deep learning fusion methods.

*   **Significance:**
    *   The reported improvements in quantitative metrics and qualitative results, if robust, indicate a significant advance in MMIF performance. Highlighting specific improvements against leading MMIF methods is important.
    *   The generalization performance on MRI-PET and PC-GFP datasets, without retraining, suggests a model with good robustness and adaptability.
    *   The ablation studies provide evidence supporting the design choices, strengthening the significance of the proposed architecture.
    *   The code availability enhances the reproducibility and impact of the work, allowing others to build upon it.
    *   However, some areas need scrutiny: The improvement gained by automatically tuning the loss functions might not be significant. A statistical test is required to highlight if the proposed approach is better than the manually tuned loss functions. The improvement margin should be statistically significant to prove that the proposed method is indeed significant.

**Strengths:**

*   Well-structured and clearly written.
*   Comprehensive experiments, including quantitative and qualitative evaluations.
*   Ablation studies to validate the design choices.
*   Generalization performance on different datasets.
*   Addresses a significant problem in medical image analysis.
*   Code availability promotes reproducibility.
*   The discussion is fairly comprehensive.

**Weaknesses:**

*   The choice of evaluation metrics could be more justified. While commonly used, some metrics are known to have limitations. Discussing the pros and cons of different metrics in the context of MMIF would strengthen the evaluation.
*   While the results look promising, a deeper investigation into the robustness of the model against different noise levels and image artifacts would be beneficial.
*   The computational cost (FLOPs) is relatively high compared to some methods. This limits its practical applicability. The authors should clearly outline the trade-offs between improved performance and increased computational demands.
*   Statistical tests are required to show that the automatic tuning of loss functions is better than the manually tuned loss functions.

**Potential Influence:**

The MMIF-AMIN architecture, particularly the adaptive loss function and integration of IDN and MCFEM, could influence future research in MMIF. The emphasis on lossless feature extraction and capturing complementary information is a promising direction.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:** The paper introduces a novel and well-designed MMIF architecture that demonstrates strong performance and generalization capabilities. The adaptive loss function addresses a key limitation of current methods. The weaknesses lie in the computational cost, the depth of the robustness analysis, and the lack of statistical testing. While promising, further validation and optimization are needed to fully realize the potential of MMIF-AMIN for practical medical applications, as well as highlighting the statistical significance between using automatic tuning vs manually tuning the loss functions.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Expert-Guided Diffusion Planner for Auto-bidding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08687v1)
- **Authors**: Yunshan Peng, Wenzheng Shu, Jiahao Sun, Yanxiang Zeng, Jinan Pang, Wentao Bai, Yunke Bai, Xialong Liu, Peng Jiang
- **Abstract**: Auto-bidding is extensively applied in advertising systems, serving a multitude of advertisers. Generative bidding is gradually gaining traction due to its robust planning capabilities and generalizability. In contrast to traditional reinforcement learning-based bidding, generative bidding does not rely on the Markov Decision Process (MDP) exhibiting superior planning capabilities in long-horizon scenarios. Conditional diffusion modeling approaches have demonstrated significant potential in the realm of auto-bidding. However, relying solely on return as the optimality condition is weak to guarantee the generation of genuinely optimal decision sequences, lacking personalized structural information. Moreover, diffusion models' t-step autoregressive generation mechanism inherently carries timeliness risks. To address these issues, we propose a novel conditional diffusion modeling method based on expert trajectory guidance combined with a skip-step sampling strategy to enhance generation efficiency. We have validated the effectiveness of this approach through extensive offline experiments and achieved statistically significant results in online A/B testing, achieving an increase of 11.29% in conversion and a 12.35% in revenue compared with the baseline.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Expert-Guided Diffusion Planner for Auto-bidding" introduces EGDB, a novel approach for auto-bidding in online advertising. It leverages a conditional diffusion model guided by expert trajectories to improve bidding performance. The key contributions are:

1.  **Expert Trajectory Guidance:**  Using expert demonstrations to provide personalized structural information for the diffusion process, addressing the limitations of relying solely on cumulative return.
2.  **Blended-Forcing Mechanism:**  A combination of teacher-forcing and VAE-enhanced decode-forcing to ensure training-inference consistency and enable exploration beyond observed demonstrations.
3.  **Dual-Conditioned Planner:** Implicitly incorporating expert behavioral semantics while explicitly enforcing domain constraints, leading to better adaptation to dynamic auction environments.
4.  **Skip-Step Sampling:**  Reducing inference steps without sacrificing action generation quality to meet real-time bidding latency requirements.

The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of EGDB through offline experiments and online A/B testing, showing significant improvements in conversion and revenue compared to baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents several novel ideas in the context of auto-bidding. The integration of expert trajectory guidance with diffusion models is a promising approach to improve bidding strategies.  The blended forcing and skip-step sampling techniques are innovative and contribute to both the performance and practicality of the method. The dual conditioning approach is a clever way to combine expert behavior and domain constraints.  Compared to existing diffusion-based bidding methods, the expert guidance provides richer contextual information than relying purely on scalar returns.
*   **Significance:**  Auto-bidding is a crucial component of online advertising, and improvements in bidding strategies can have a significant impact on revenue and ROI.  The paper shows clear improvements in both offline experiments and real-world A/B testing.  The emphasis on real-time execution through the skip-step sampling method makes the approach more practical than many other diffusion-based methods, which can be computationally expensive.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined problem and clear motivation for the proposed approach.
    *   Innovative combination of different techniques (diffusion models, expert trajectories, VAEs, blended forcing).
    *   Strong experimental results, including offline ablation studies and online A/B testing.
    *   Focus on practical considerations, such as real-time execution and computational efficiency.
    * The paper thoroughly describes how the method solves existing issues by introducing EGDB, a novel Expert-Guided Diffusion planner for auto-Bidding.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the paper outlines a clear improvement through the proposed approach, the implementation details of the feature window, outlined at the end of the research, are limited. Additional information to the specific properties of the window (i.e sizes) would contribute more to the methodology of the paper.
    *   The reliance on expert trajectories might be a limitation in scenarios where such data is scarce or unreliable. Further discussion about the robustness of the approach to noisy or sub-optimal expert data would be valuable.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more detailed comparison with other generative auto-bidding methods, especially those based on transformers or other sequence modeling techniques.
    *   While the A/B testing shows positive results, the duration of the experiment (one week) might be relatively short. Longer-term experiments would provide more confidence in the sustained performance of the method.
    *   The paper focuses on a specific advertising environment (Alibaba's simulated environment). More discussion about the generalizability of the approach to other advertising platforms or auction mechanisms would be beneficial.
*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more effective and practical auto-bidding strategies. The proposed techniques could be adopted by advertising platforms and advertisers to improve campaign performance and ROI.  The combination of expert guidance and diffusion models could also inspire new research directions in other sequence generation tasks.

**Overall:**

The paper presents a well-motivated and technically sound approach to auto-bidding using expert-guided diffusion models. The experimental results and the focus on practical considerations make this a valuable contribution to the field. While some limitations exist, the strengths of the paper outweigh the weaknesses.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### STELAR-VISION: Self-Topology-Aware Efficient Learning for Aligned Reasoning in Vision
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08688v1)
- **Authors**: Chen Li, Han Zhang, Zhantao Yang, Fangyi Chen, Zihan Wang, Anudeepsekhar Bolimera, Marios Savvides
- **Abstract**: Vision-language models (VLMs) have made significant strides in reasoning, yet they often struggle with complex multimodal tasks and tend to generate overly verbose outputs. A key limitation is their reliance on chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning, despite many tasks benefiting from alternative topologies like trees or graphs. To address this, we introduce STELAR-Vision, a training framework for topology-aware reasoning. At its core is TopoAug, a synthetic data pipeline that enriches training with diverse topological structures. Using supervised fine-tuning and reinforcement learning, we post-train Qwen2VL models with both accuracy and efficiency in mind. Additionally, we propose Frugal Learning, which reduces output length with minimal accuracy loss. On MATH-V and VLM-S2H, STELAR-Vision improves accuracy by 9.7% over its base model and surpasses the larger Qwen2VL-72B-Instruct by 7.3%. On five out-of-distribution benchmarks, it outperforms Phi-4-Multimodal-Instruct by up to 28.4% and LLaMA-3.2-11B-Vision-Instruct by up to 13.2%, demonstrating strong generalization. Compared to Chain-Only training, our approach achieves 4.3% higher overall accuracy on in-distribution datasets and consistently outperforms across all OOD benchmarks. We have released datasets, and code will be available.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "STELAR-VISION: Self-Topology-Aware Efficient Learning for Aligned Reasoning in Vision":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces STELAR-Vision, a training framework for vision-language models (VLMs) that emphasizes topology-aware reasoning. It addresses the common tendency of VLMs to rely heavily on chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning, even when alternative reasoning topologies (trees, graphs) might be more appropriate.  STELAR-Vision utilizes a synthetic data generation pipeline called TopoAug, which creates training data with diverse topological structures and labels them based on question characteristics.  The models are then fine-tuned using supervised learning and reinforcement learning, with a focus on both accuracy and efficiency. The authors also propose Frugal Learning to promote concise responses. Experiments demonstrate improved accuracy on MATH-V and VLM_S2H datasets and enhanced generalization on out-of-distribution benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of topology-aware reasoning is reasonably novel in the context of *vision-language models*. While different reasoning structures (ToT, GoT) have been explored in language-only models, the automatic generation (TopoAug) and alignment of reasoning topologies with question characteristics in a *vision*-based setting is a distinctive contribution. The idea of Frugal Learning, though inspired by efficiency efforts in standard LLMs, also adds value by connecting the idea with the generated reasoning topologies.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work lies in several areas.
    *   It offers a practical training methodology (STELAR-Vision) to improve VLM reasoning by diversifying the kind of examples it sees during training.
    *   It reveals limitations of relying solely on CoT reasoning in VLMs and advocates for a more flexible approach.
    *   It highlights the potential for enhancing efficiency (shorter responses) by leveraging different reasoning topologies.
    *   The empirical results support these claims by showing improvements in both accuracy and generalization compared to existing models and CoT-only baselines. The OOD performance is particularly strong.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the proposed framework and the reasoning behind it.
    *   TopoAug is a key strength. The authors use a method to automatically generate a variety of synthetic reasoning examples that match the strengths of the base model.
    *   The use of both supervised fine-tuning and reinforcement learning is a good combination, allowing for both learning from diverse data and aligning the model with desired behavior.
    *   The experimental evaluation is comprehensive, including in-distribution and out-of-distribution datasets and comparisons with strong baselines.
    *   The ablation studies provide insights into the effectiveness of different components of the framework.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on synthetic data could introduce biases or artifacts that limit the model's real-world performance. The quality and diversity of TopoAug are critical, but it's difficult to fully assess without detailed analysis of the generated data.
    *   While the Frugal Learning aspect is interesting, the performance of STELAR-Vision-Short+ is inconsistent. This suggests that the approach to penalizing lengthy outputs might need further refinement.
    *   While comparing against strong baselines, the paper could benefit from more in-depth analysis of why certain topologies are better suited for specific types of questions. Understanding this relationship could lead to even more effective topology-aware reasoning.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in VLM training, particularly in the areas of reasoning and efficiency. The concept of topology-aware reasoning could be adopted in other multimodal tasks beyond mathematical and logical reasoning. Additionally, the TopoAug pipeline could serve as a valuable tool for generating diverse training data for VLMs. Future works could explore different generation models and prompt engineering for TopoAug, explore better designs to penalize length in Frugal Learning, or propose better designs to assign labels.

**Score:** 7.5

**Rationale:** The paper presents a good approach and solid results. TopoAug is also an interesting direction and leads to performance improvements in general, which suggests the idea is valuable. While it builds upon existing ideas in LLMs (CoT, ToT, GoT, efficient training), it makes a valuable contribution to the specific domain of vision-language models. The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach. However, the reliance on synthetic data and the limitations of the Frugal Learning aspect prevent it from receiving a higher score. Future work should focus on these limitations and explore the full potential of topology-aware reasoning in VLMs.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### DiffVolume: Diffusion Models for Volume Generation in Limit Order Books
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08698v1)
- **Authors**: Zhuohan Wang, Carmine Ventre
- **Abstract**: Modeling limit order books (LOBs) dynamics is a fundamental problem in market microstructure research. In particular, generating high-dimensional volume snapshots with strong temporal and liquidity-dependent patterns remains a challenging task, despite recent work exploring the application of Generative Adversarial Networks to LOBs. In this work, we propose a conditional \textbf{Diff}usion model for the generation of future LOB \textbf{Volume} snapshots (\textbf{DiffVolume}). We evaluate our model across three axes: (1) \textit{Realism}, where we show that DiffVolume, conditioned on past volume history and time of day, better reproduces statistical properties such as marginal distribution, spatial correlation, and autocorrelation decay; (2) \textit{Counterfactual generation}, allowing for controllable generation under hypothetical liquidity scenarios by additionally conditioning on a target future liquidity profile; and (3) \textit{Downstream prediction}, where we show that the synthetic counterfactual data from our model improves the performance of future liquidity forecasting models. Together, these results suggest that DiffVolume provides a powerful and flexible framework for realistic and controllable LOB volume generation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DiffVolume, a novel diffusion model for generating future Limit Order Book (LOB) volume snapshots. The model is conditioned on historical order flow, time of day, and, optionally, target future liquidity levels. The authors demonstrate that DiffVolume can generate realistic LOB volumes, supports counterfactual generation under different liquidity scenarios, and improves the performance of short-term liquidity prediction when used to augment real data. They evaluate the model across multiple stocks and use various metrics to assess realism and predictive performance, comparing it against GAN-based methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The application of diffusion models to LOB volume generation is a significant contribution. While diffusion models are increasingly popular in various domains, their application to financial market microstructure, particularly for generating LOB data with a focus on volume and liquidity, is relatively novel. The specific architecture of DiffVolume, incorporating price level embeddings, time step embeddings, and conditioning contexts via FiLM, is also a meaningful technical contribution tailored to the structure of LOB data.

*   **Significance:** Accurate modeling of LOB dynamics is crucial for market microstructure research and financial applications. DiffVolume offers a potential improvement over existing generative models, especially GANs, which suffer from training instability and mode collapse. The ability to generate counterfactual LOB scenarios is particularly valuable for stress-testing trading strategies and understanding market behavior under different liquidity conditions. The demonstration of improved liquidity prediction, a vital task for traders and risk managers, further enhances the practical significance of the work.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Realistic Generation:** The paper presents strong evidence that DiffVolume generates more realistic LOB volumes compared to GANs, based on marginal distribution, spatial correlation, and autocorrelation decay analyses.
    *   **Controllability:** The ability to generate counterfactual scenarios by conditioning on target liquidity profiles is a key strength, allowing for controlled experimentation and analysis.
    *   **Downstream Utility:** Demonstrating improved liquidity prediction performance with synthetically augmented data validates the practical value of DiffVolume.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper provides a comprehensive evaluation using multiple stocks, metrics, and comparison models.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Cost:** Diffusion models can be computationally expensive to train and sample from. The paper does not explicitly discuss the computational resources required for DiffVolume.
    *   **Limited Data:** The model is trained and tested on a relatively short time period (16 trading days for training). While the paper acknowledges the non-stationary nature of LOB data and takes steps to mitigate its impact, it is unclear how the model would perform over longer and more diverse market conditions.
    *   **Comparisons:** While the model outperforms a GAN baseline, it would be beneficial to compare with more recent state-of-the-art generative models in the LOB domain, if available.
    *   **Parameter Sensitivity:** The paper lacks any details about the parameter tuning done for the diffusion model. The sensitivity of the results to hyperparameters and diffusion sampling scheme needs to be explored.

*   **Potential Influence:** DiffVolume has the potential to influence research in market microstructure by providing a more reliable and controllable generative model for LOB data. It could be used for stress-testing trading algorithms, simulating market events, and improving risk management techniques. The general approach could also be extended to other financial time series data.

**Justification of Score:**

Considering both the strengths and weaknesses, a score of 7 is warranted. The application of diffusion models to LOB volume generation is novel and potentially impactful, but there are some limitations to consider. The computational cost is not discussed, and the data is relatively limited. Furthermore, comparisons to more advanced LOB generative models would solidify its position. However, the thorough evaluation and positive results on realism, controllability, and downstream utility contribute to its significance. The results provide sufficient validation of the new approach to provide a baseline for future work and thus, is a positive contribution to the field.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: q-fin.TR
- **Score**: 7/10

### SafeFix: Targeted Model Repair via Controlled Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08701v1)
- **Authors**: Ouyang Xu, Baoming Zhang, Ruiyu Mao, Yunhui Guo
- **Abstract**: Deep learning models for visual recognition often exhibit systematic errors due to underrepresented semantic subpopulations. Although existing debugging frameworks can pinpoint these failures by identifying key failure attributes, repairing the model effectively remains difficult.
