- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Scaling Learned Image Compression Models up to 1 Billion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.09075v1)
- **Authors**: Yuqi Li, Haotian Zhang, Li Li, Dong Liu, Feng Wu
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in large language models (LLMs) highlight a strong connection between intelligence and compression. Learned image compression, a fundamental task in modern data compression, has made significant progress in recent years. However, current models remain limited in scale, restricting their representation capacity, and how scaling model size influences compression performance remains unexplored. In this work, we present a pioneering study on scaling up learned image compression models and revealing the performance trends through scaling laws. Using the recent state-of-the-art HPCM model as baseline, we scale model parameters from 68.5 millions to 1 billion and fit power-law relations between test loss and key scaling variables, including model size and optimal training compute. The results reveal a scaling trend, enabling extrapolation to larger scale models. Experimental results demonstrate that the scaled-up HPCM-1B model achieves state-of-the-art rate-distortion performance. We hope this work inspires future exploration of large-scale compression models and deeper investigations into the connection between compression and intelligence.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper presents a study on scaling up learned image compression models. The authors scale the parameters of a hierarchical progressive context modeling (HPCM) framework from 68.5 million to 1 billion parameters. They analyze the relationship between model size, training compute, and compression performance, revealing power-law scaling relationships. The results demonstrate that larger models achieve better rate-distortion performance, with the HPCM-1B model achieving state-of-the-art results. The authors extrapolate the scaling trends to estimate the performance of even larger models and discuss the potential link between compression and intelligence. They also explored transformer-based architectures but found unstable convergence and higher test loss compared to the convolution-based architecture.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in being the first empirical study to investigate scaling laws in learned image compression. While scaling laws have been extensively studied in language models and vision models, they were unexplored in learned image compression. The exploration of scaling up image compression models is new. Although the base architecture (HPCM) is not new, the work demonstrates that it can be effectively scaled. The attempt to explore transformer based models and the documentation of challenges there is useful, although not entirely successful from an empirical perspective.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from several aspects:
    *   **State-of-the-Art Performance:** The HPCM-1B model achieves state-of-the-art rate-distortion performance, demonstrating the potential of large-scale models in image compression.
    *   **Scaling Laws:** The discovery of scaling laws in learned image compression provides valuable insights into how model size and training compute affect performance. This knowledge can guide future research and development of larger and more efficient compression models.
    *   **Connection to Intelligence:** The paper links compression to intelligence, which is a thought-provoking idea that could inspire further research into the fundamental nature of intelligence and the role of compression.
    *   **Practical Benchmark:** This work provides a practical benchmark that can be used by the community.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The paper presents a comprehensive set of experiments, including model scaling, scaling law analysis, and performance evaluation on standard datasets.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and easy to understand.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The authors make their model implementation publicly available, promoting reproducibility and further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Model Generality:** The scaling analysis is primarily based on the HPCM architecture. Exploring scaling trends in other model designs would strengthen the study.
    *   **Model Scale Granularity:** The scaling law curves are fitted using only five model sizes, which could limit the accuracy of the estimates. More models at different scales would be helpful.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Transformers:** The exploration of transformer-based architectures is preliminary and doesn't yield positive results. More in-depth analysis of why transformers don't perform well in this context would be valuable.
    *   **Computational cost:** The computational cost may be high, limiting accessibility.

* **Potential influence** The findings will be influential in driving research efforts toward larger image compression models as the hardware develops to allow it. It sets the baseline for future researchers.

**Justification for the Score:**

Overall, this paper presents a significant contribution to the field of learned image compression. The discovery of scaling laws is novel and valuable, and the HPCM-1B model achieves state-of-the-art performance. The limitations related to model generality, scaling granularity, and limited transformer exploration are important, but they do not diminish the overall impact of the paper. The strong connections to existing research, detailed methodologies, and compelling results warrant a high score.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Dynamic Uncertainty-aware Multimodal Fusion for Outdoor Health Monitoring
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.09085v1)
- **Authors**: Zihan Fang, Zheng Lin, Senkang Hu, Yihang Tao, Yiqin Deng, Xianhao Chen, Yuguang Fang
- **Abstract**: Outdoor health monitoring is essential to detect early abnormal health status for safeguarding human health and safety. Conventional outdoor monitoring relies on static multimodal deep learning frameworks, which requires extensive data training from scratch and fails to capture subtle health status changes. Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) emerge as a promising alternative, utilizing only small datasets to fine-tune pre-trained information-rich models for enabling powerful health status monitoring. Unfortunately, MLLM-based outdoor health monitoring also faces significant challenges: I) sensor data contains input noise stemming from sensor data acquisition and fluctuation noise caused by sudden changes in physiological signals due to dynamic outdoor environments, thus degrading the training performance; ii) current transformer based MLLMs struggle to achieve robust multimodal fusion, as they lack a design for fusing the noisy modality; iii) modalities with varying noise levels hinder accurate recovery of missing data from fluctuating distributions. To combat these challenges, we propose an uncertainty-aware multimodal fusion framework, named DUAL-Health, for outdoor health monitoring in dynamic and noisy environments. First, to assess the impact of noise, we accurately quantify modality uncertainty caused by input and fluctuation noise with current and temporal features. Second, to empower efficient muitimodal fusion with low-quality modalities,we customize the fusion weight for each modality based on quantified and calibrated uncertainty. Third, to enhance data recovery from fluctuating noisy modalities, we align modality distributions within a common semantic space. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our DUAL-Health outperforms state-of-the-art baselines in detection accuracy and robustness.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DUAL-Health, a novel framework for outdoor health monitoring designed to handle noisy and dynamic environments. It addresses challenges faced by multimodal large language models (MLLMs) in this context, specifically: i) input noise from sensors and physiological signal fluctuations; ii) robust multimodal fusion with noisy modalities; and iii) accurate recovery of missing data. DUAL-Health employs three core components: modality uncertainty quantification using current and temporal features, a transformer-based multimodal fusion that adjusts fusion weights based on uncertainty, and a missing modality reconstruction module aligning modality distributions within a common semantic space. The paper presents experimental results demonstrating DUAL-Health's superior performance compared to state-of-the-art baselines in detection accuracy and robustness.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper presents several novel aspects.  The most significant is the comprehensive approach to uncertainty, explicitly modeling both *input noise* and *fluctuation noise* from physiological changes. Distinguishing these two noise sources is crucial for accurate health monitoring, and this separation is not typically addressed in existing literature. The dynamic adjustment of fusion weights and cross-modal attention based on uncertainty quantification, tailored for transformer architectures, is also a significant contribution. Furthermore, transferring modality distributions to a common semantic space for modality reconstruction is a unique and potentially valuable technique. However, some components such as the use of transformers and autoencoders, are standard. It is the specific configuration and adaptation to the outdoor health monitoring application that demonstrates novelty.

* **Significance:** Outdoor health monitoring is a vital area with potential for significant societal impact. The ability to accurately detect health emergencies in dynamic and noisy environments is a major challenge. This paper directly addresses this challenge by improving the robustness and accuracy of MLLMs for this application. The potential benefits are substantial, including earlier detection of cardiovascular events, falls, and other health crises. Moreover, the framework's ability to handle missing data is important in practical outdoor settings. The ablation studies further confirm the efficacy of each sub-module, bolstering the credibility of the novel contribution.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Problem Definition:**  Clearly articulates the challenges specific to outdoor health monitoring that are not adequately addressed by existing methods.
    * **Comprehensive Approach:**  The integrated approach of uncertainty quantification, dynamic fusion, and missing modality reconstruction is well-conceived.
    * **Experimental Evaluation:** Extensive experiments demonstrating superior performance against strong baselines on relevant datasets (Stressors and UP-Fall).
    * **Ablation Studies:**  Detailed ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each module within DUAL-Health. This enhances trustworthiness and understanding.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Dataset limitations**: The Stressors and UP-Fall datasets, while relevant, may not fully capture the full spectrum of real-world scenarios encountered in outdoor health monitoring (e.g., diverse environmental conditions, variations in sensor quality, and the complexity of human behavior). A dataset with diverse environments should be constructed.
    * **Computational cost**: The computational complexity of the proposed approach compared to the baselines may not be clear. This would be valuable for deployment on resource-constrained edge devices.
    * **Generalizability**: The approach is heavily tailored to specific modalities (physiological signals, facial expressions). The paper could benefit from discussing the generalizability of the framework to other modalities and applications.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of multimodal health monitoring. The methods for uncertainty quantification and dynamic fusion could be adopted by other researchers working on similar problems. The explicit modeling of input and fluctuation noise is a key contribution that could lead to more robust and reliable systems. Furthermore, the approach to missing modality reconstruction could inspire new techniques for handling incomplete data in other applications.

**Justification of Score:**

DUAL-Health represents a significant advance in MLLM-based health monitoring, particularly for challenging outdoor environments. The careful modeling of different noise sources, the dynamic fusion strategy, and the novel approach to modality reconstruction contribute to enhanced robustness and accuracy. The extensive experimental validation provides strong evidence of the framework's effectiveness. While there are some limitations regarding the specific datasets and computational costs, the potential for impact is substantial. The key lies in a framework designed to enhance the capabilities of MLLMs, enabling a nuanced approach to multimodal fusion. This framework has the potential to improve the reliability and accuracy of outdoor health monitoring systems.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.NI
- **Score**: 8/10

### Utilizing Multilingual Encoders to Improve Large Language Models for Low-Resource Languages
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.09091v1)
- **Authors**: Imalsha Puranegedara, Themira Chathumina, Nisal Ranathunga, Nisansa de Silva, Surangika Ranathunga, Mokanarangan Thayaparan
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) excel in English, but their performance degrades significantly on low-resource languages (LRLs) due to English-centric training. While methods like LangBridge align LLMs with multilingual encoders such as the Massively Multilingual Text-to-Text Transfer Transformer (mT5), they typically use only the final encoder layer. We propose a novel architecture that fuses all intermediate layers, enriching the linguistic information passed to the LLM. Our approach features two strategies: (1) a Global Softmax weighting for overall layer importance, and (2) a Transformer Softmax model that learns token-specific weights. The fused representations are mapped into the LLM's embedding space, enabling it to process multilingual inputs. The model is trained only on English data, without using any parallel or multilingual data. Evaluated on XNLI, IndicXNLI, Sinhala News Classification, and Amazon Reviews, our Transformer Softmax model significantly outperforms the LangBridge baseline. We observe strong performance gains in LRLs, improving Sinhala classification accuracy from 71.66% to 75.86% and achieving clear improvements across Indic languages such as Tamil, Bengali, and Malayalam. These specific gains contribute to an overall boost in average XNLI accuracy from 70.36% to 71.50%. This approach offers a scalable, data-efficient path toward more capable and equitable multilingual LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of Large Language Models (LLMs) performing poorly on low-resource languages (LRLs) due to their English-centric training.  The authors propose a novel architecture that fuses all intermediate layers of a multilingual encoder (mT5) into an English-centric LLM (Llama 3.2 1B Instruct) to improve performance on LRLs. The approach features two strategies: Global Softmax weighting for overall layer importance and a Transformer Softmax model for learning token-specific weights.  The fused representations are mapped into the LLM's embedding space. The model is trained only on English data and evaluated in a zero-shot setting on XNLI, IndicXNLI, Sinhala News Classification, and Amazon Reviews datasets. Results demonstrate significant improvements in LRLs compared to the LangBridge baseline, with particular gains in languages with scripts similar to English. The authors emphasize the method's data efficiency and scalability for creating more capable and equitable multilingual LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The core novelty lies in the *fusion of all layers* of a multilingual encoder, specifically learning weights for these layers (either globally or token-wise) to improve the performance of an English-centric LLM on LRLs in a zero-shot setting. Previous approaches like LangBridge typically use only the final layer, potentially losing valuable linguistic information encoded in the intermediate layers. The token-wise softmax weighting is a further refinement, allowing for dynamic selection of relevant layers based on the input token.

While layer fusion has been explored in other contexts (e.g., monolingual NMT, English classification), applying it to the specific problem of improving zero-shot transfer to LRLs in LLMs with a focus on multilingual encoders represents a significant advancement.  The use of a trainable temperature parameter to control the sharpness of the softmax attention is also a minor but useful contribution.

**Significance:**

The paper's significance stems from its potential to improve the accessibility and performance of LLMs for a wider range of languages.  The data-efficient, zero-shot transfer approach is particularly valuable for LRLs where annotated data is scarce.  The empirical results demonstrate a tangible improvement in performance on several LRL benchmarks, suggesting that the proposed architecture can effectively leverage cross-lingual representations.  Furthermore, the interpretability aspect (analyzing layer weight distributions) provides insights into the model's inner workings and could guide further research.  The observed improvements in languages with similar scripts to English also highlight a practical benefit for transfer learning in related languages. The ablation studies provided in the paper would also benefit the overall research.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear problem definition:** The paper clearly articulates the performance gap in LLMs for LRLs.
*   **Novel method:** The layer fusion approach and the token-wise weighting mechanism are well-motivated and innovative.
*   **Strong empirical results:**  The results demonstrate significant improvements over a strong baseline (LangBridge) across multiple datasets and LRLs.
*   **Data efficiency:**  The model is trained only on English data, making it highly practical for LRL settings.
*   **Interpretability:** The analysis of layer weight distributions provides valuable insights.
*   **Reproducibility:** The paper clearly describes the experimental setup and training details.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Model Size:** The experiments are limited to a relatively small LLM (Llama 3.2 1B Instruct). It's unclear how well the proposed method would scale to larger, more powerful models like the current state-of-the-art LLMs.  The authors acknowledge this limitation, but it is still a critical consideration.
*   **Multilingual-Tuned LLMs:** The paper compares the method to LangBridge but doesn't compare against LLMs that are pre-trained with multilingual data. It would also be a beneficial study to compare the approach with different pre-trained LLMs (Mistral, Gemma, etc.) or multilingual-tuned models, because it will also showcase what can be gained on already-multilingual tuned LLMs.
*   **Limited analysis of edge cases or failure modes:**  While the average performance improves, the paper doesn't delve deeply into the specific types of errors the model still makes on LRLs.  Analyzing these cases could provide further insights into the limitations of the approach and suggest areas for improvement.
*   **Computational Cost comparison:** It would be helpful to provide a comparison of the computational cost of the token-wise softmax to the global softmax approach.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of multilingual LLMs. The method is novel, well-motivated, and demonstrates significant improvements in performance for LRLs.  While the limited model size and the absence of comparison against multilingual LLMs are weaknesses, the data efficiency and interpretability of the approach are strong points. The zero-shot transfer capability is particularly valuable for addressing the challenges of LRLs. Therefore, I am assigning it a score of 7. This score reflects the paper's significant contribution to the field, while acknowledging its limitations regarding scalability and comparative analysis with larger and already multilingual models.
Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Scaling Up Active Testing to Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.09093v1)
- **Authors**: Gabrielle Berrada, Jannik Kossen, Muhammed Razzak, Freddie Bickford Smith, Yarin Gal, Tom Rainforth
- **Abstract**: Active testing enables label-efficient evaluation of models through careful data acquisition. However, its significant computational costs have previously undermined its use for large models. We show how it can be successfully scaled up to the evaluation of large language models (LLMs). In particular we show that the surrogate model used to guide data acquisition can be constructed cheaply using in-context learning, does not require updating within an active-testing loop, and can be smaller than the target model. We even find we can make good data-acquisition decisions without computing predictions with the target model and further introduce a single-run error estimator to asses how well active testing is working on the fly. We find that our approach is able to more effectively evaluate LLM performance with less data than current standard practices.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of efficiently evaluating Large Language Models (LLMs) using active testing.  Active testing aims to reduce labeling costs by strategically selecting the most informative data points for labeling.  However, the computational demands of active testing, particularly the repeated training and prediction steps involving surrogate models, often hinder its application to large models. The paper proposes several strategies to overcome these bottlenecks: (1) using in-context learning to construct a cheap, fixed surrogate model without iterative training, (2) employing smaller surrogate models compared to the target LLM, and (3) approximating target model predictions during data acquisition using solely the surrogate model's predictions. Furthermore, the paper introduces a bootstrap-based estimator to assess the quality of the risk-estimation *during* a single active testing run, addressing a practical deployment concern.  Empirical results demonstrate significant improvements over random sampling in estimating model performance with reduced labeling effort.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its specific combination of existing techniques to make active testing feasible for LLMs, a domain where computational costs are a primary concern.  While the individual components (active testing, in-context learning, smaller surrogates, risk estimation) are not entirely new, their adaptation and integration to address the unique challenges of LLM evaluation are novel.  The bootstrap estimator is a valuable practical addition.
* **Significance:**  The paper contributes significantly to the practical application of active testing.  LLM evaluation is expensive and a bottleneck in LLM development.  The presented approach directly reduces the cost of evaluation, making it more accessible and scalable. The ability to assess the trustworthiness of a single active-testing run is also very impactful for real-world use.  The empirical results are strong, showing substantial improvements in risk estimation over random sampling.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Practical Focus:** The paper is very focused on addressing the *practical* challenges of scaling active testing to LLMs.  The proposed solutions are relatively straightforward to implement and demonstrably effective.
    * **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the computational bottlenecks in the active testing pipeline and provides targeted solutions.
    * **Strong Empirical Validation:** The experimental evaluation is comprehensive, covering a variety of datasets, models, and settings.  The comparative analysis against random sampling provides a solid baseline for assessing the effectiveness of the proposed approach.
    * **Novel and Practically useful Risk-Estimation Error Assessment:** The proposed and evaluated error estimate can significantly improve the usability of active testing, as it allows practicioners to assess the "quality" of an active-testing run, which is in contrast to most prior work that presents active testing in a setting where many runs are possible and one can rely on classical error bounds (or sample many times).

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Theoretical Analysis:** The paper relies primarily on empirical validation. While the bootstrap estimator is introduced, a more in-depth theoretical analysis of its convergence and properties would strengthen the contribution.  More discussion on the limits and failure cases of surrogate model approximation would be helpful.
    * **Dependence on Surrogate Model Quality:** While the paper demonstrates that smaller and fixed surrogates can be effective, the overall performance remains dependent on the surrogate model's quality. The paper discusses this and mentions how it fails gracefully as the surrogate quality degrades.
    * **Focus on Classification:** The paper focuses mainly on text classification tasks.  The generalizability of the proposed approach to other LLM evaluation scenarios (e.g., text generation, code generation) is not fully explored.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of LLM evaluation by making active testing a more viable option. The practical insights and cost-saving measures presented in the paper could encourage wider adoption of active testing techniques.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a well-motivated and empirically validated solution to a crucial problem in LLM evaluation. The practical focus and ease of implementation are significant strengths. While lacking some theoretical depth and demonstrating results only for text classification and strong dependence on some sort of sensible surrogate model, the paper's overall novelty and impact are substantial and justify a high score. The significant gains over random sampling, especially when considering label efficiency, coupled with the introduction of a diagnostic tool for assessing single-run quality, position this paper as a valuable contribution to the field. Therefore, a score of 8 reflects a strong contribution but also acknowledges the identified limitations that future work could address.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### Bridging Formal Language with Chain-of-Thought Reasoning to Geometry Problem Solving
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.09099v1)
- **Authors**: Tianyun Yang, Yunwen Li, Ziniu Li, Zhihang Lin, Ruoyu Sun, Tian Ding
- **Abstract**: Large vision language models exhibit notable limitations on Geometry Problem Solving (GPS) because of their unreliable diagram interpretation and pure natural-language reasoning. A recent line of work mitigates this by using symbolic solvers: the model directly generates a formal program that a geometry solver can execute. However, this direct program generation lacks intermediate reasoning, making the decision process opaque and prone to errors. In this work, we explore a new approach that integrates Chain-of-Thought (CoT) with formal language. The model interleaves natural language reasoning with incremental emission of solver-executable code, producing a hybrid reasoning trace in which critical derivations are expressed in formal language. To teach this behavior at scale, we combine (1) supervised fine-tuning on an 11K newly developed synthetic dataset with interleaved natural language reasoning and automatic formalization, and (2) solver-in-the-loop reinforcement learning that jointly optimizes both the CoT narrative and the resulting program through outcome-based rewards. Built on Qwen2.5-VL-7B, our new model, named GF-Reasoner, achieves up to 15% accuracy improvements on standard GPS benchmarks, surpassing both 7B-scale peers and the much larger model Qwen2.5-VL-72B. By exploiting high-order geometric knowledge and offloading symbolic computation to the solver, the generated reasoning traces are noticeably shorter and cleaner. Furthermore, we present a comprehensive analysis of method design choices (e.g., reasoning paradigms, data synthesis, training epochs, etc.), providing actionable insights for future research.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the limitations of Large Vision Language Models (LVLMs) in Geometry Problem Solving (GPS).  LVLMs often struggle with unreliable diagram interpretation and pure natural language reasoning. The authors propose a new approach, GF-Reasoner, that integrates Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning with formal language. The model interleaves natural language and solver-executable code, creating a hybrid reasoning trace where derivations are formalized. This is achieved through supervised fine-tuning on a new synthetic dataset and solver-in-the-loop reinforcement learning.  The GF-Reasoner, built on Qwen2.5-VL-7B, demonstrates significant accuracy improvements on GPS benchmarks, surpassing both 7B-scale peers and the larger Qwen 2.5-VL-72B model. The authors also present an analysis of design choices.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of integrating CoT with formal language within GPS is relatively novel. Prior work primarily relied on either pure natural language reasoning or direct generation of formal programs.  The hybrid approach allows for leveraging the strengths of both paradigms: natural language for high-level understanding and formal language for precise computation and geometric reasoning. The generation of the new 11k-sample dataset is also a valuable contribution. The bidirectional synthesis method for dataset creation is a sensible approach to boost the utilization of the readily available information. However, the novelty of SFT and RL is incremental and not particularly significant. They're used in a fairly standard way here.
* **Significance:**  The performance improvements are compelling. The accuracy gains of up to 15% compared to state-of-the-art models, including larger ones (72B), signify a substantial advance in LVLM-based GPS.  The demonstrated token efficiency is another important aspect, indicating that the model achieves better performance with less verbose reasoning traces, suggesting more focused and relevant reasoning. The ablation studies contribute valuable insights into data synthesis, training paradigms, and the importance of moderate SFT. This will likely influence future research. However, the paper still contains limitations. The framework still can not handle construction problems, such as adding auxiliary lines. The current RL training process receives limited supervision, limiting its performance.
* **Strengths:**
    * Clear problem statement and well-defined approach.
    * Significant empirical results demonstrating performance gains.
    * Comprehensive analysis of design choices, providing actionable insights.
    * The creation of a new, sizable dataset addressing a key data scarcity problem.
    * Effective integration of multiple techniques (SFT, RL, hybrid reasoning).
* **Weaknesses:**
    * The reliance on a synthetic dataset raises concerns about generalizability to more complex real-world GPS problems.
    * The paper mainly focused on a single dataset PGPS9K, limiting its evaluation.
    * Although the approach shows promising results, it is still far from perfect. The framework can not handle diagrammatic construction problems.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a well-executed idea with demonstrable benefits in a challenging area. The innovation of integrating CoT with formal language, although not entirely groundbreaking, significantly improves GPS performance and token efficiency. The comprehensive analysis and the generation of the new dataset are additional strengths. However, the reliance on a synthetic dataset, the limitations in handling more complex problems (e.g., requiring auxiliary constructions), and the still imperfect performance detract from the overall impact. The paper advances the state-of-the-art but has areas where it can be expanded upon. For these reasons, a score in the upper-middle range is warranted.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### AutoCodeBench: Large Language Models are Automatic Code Benchmark Generators
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.09101v1)
- **Authors**: Jason Chou, Ao Liu, Yuchi Deng, Zhiying Zeng, Tao Zhang, Haotian Zhu, Jianwei Cai, Yue Mao, Chenchen Zhang, Lingyun Tan, Ziyan Xu, Bohui Zhai, Hengyi Liu, Speed Zhu, Wiggin Zhou, Fengzong Lian
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated remarkable capabilities across various domains, with code generation emerging as a key area of focus. While numerous benchmarks have been proposed to evaluate their code generation abilities, these benchmarks face several critical limitations. First, they often rely on manual annotations, which are time-consuming and difficult to scale across different programming languages and problem complexities. Second, most existing benchmarks focus primarily on Python, while the few multilingual benchmarks suffer from limited difficulty and uneven language distribution. To address these challenges, we propose AutoCodeGen, an automated method for generating high-difficulty multilingual code generation datasets without manual annotations. AutoCodeGen ensures the correctness and completeness of test cases by generating test inputs with LLMs and obtaining test outputs through a multilingual sandbox, while achieving high data quality through reverse-order problem generation and multiple filtering steps. Using this novel method, we introduce AutoCodeBench, a large-scale code generation benchmark comprising 3,920 problems evenly distributed across 20 programming languages. It is specifically designed to evaluate LLMs on challenging, diverse, and practical multilingual tasks. We evaluate over 30 leading open-source and proprietary LLMs on AutoCodeBench and its simplified version AutoCodeBench-Lite. The results show that even the most advanced LLMs struggle with the complexity, diversity, and multilingual nature of these tasks. Besides, we introduce AutoCodeBench-Complete, specifically designed for base models to assess their few-shot code generation capabilities. We hope the AutoCodeBench series will serve as a valuable resource and inspire the community to focus on more challenging and practical multilingual code generation scenarios.
- **Summary**: This paper introduces AutoCodeBench, an automated framework for generating high-difficulty, multilingual code generation benchmarks. The authors address the limitations of existing benchmarks, which often rely on manual annotations, are primarily focused on Python, and lack sufficient diversity and complexity. AutoCodeGen leverages LLM-Sandbox interaction, ensuring correctness and completeness of test cases by generating inputs with LLMs and validating outputs through a multilingual sandbox. Reverse-order problem generation and multiple filtering steps enhance data quality. The result is AutoCodeBench, a large-scale benchmark comprising 3,920 problems across 20 programming languages. The paper also presents evaluations of over 30 LLMs on AutoCodeBench and its simplified version, AutoCodeBench-Lite, revealing challenges in handling complex, diverse, and multilingual tasks. Additionally, AutoCodeBench-Complete is introduced for base model evaluation. A multilingual sandbox supporting 20+ languages is also open-sourced.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

The paper addresses a relevant and pressing need in the field of code generation: the lack of challenging and diverse benchmarks suitable for evaluating modern LLMs. The automated approach to benchmark generation is a significant step forward, potentially alleviating the burden of manual annotation and enabling continuous benchmark evolution.

**Strengths:**

*   **Automation:** The key strength is the automated generation process, making benchmark creation more scalable and less resource-intensive.
*   **Multilingual Support:** The benchmark's coverage of 20 programming languages is a significant improvement over existing, mostly Python-centric, benchmarks.
*   **Difficulty:** The focus on generating high-difficulty problems is well-justified, given the rapid advancements in LLM capabilities.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper provides a thorough evaluation of numerous LLMs across different configurations.
*   **Resource Release:** The open-sourcing of the multilingual sandbox is a valuable contribution to the community.
*   **Multi-Logical Problem Generation:** Generation of code problems that require LLMs to address multi-functional solutions.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **LLM Dependency:** The entire framework relies on LLMs for generation and filtering. This introduces potential bias towards LLMs used during the generation process and limitations in the capabilities that the generation pipeline can produce. Although the author addresses this concern of 'bias' to a degree, there remain some open questions regarding to what degree this is the case.
*   **Quality of Generated Problems:** While the authors implement several filtering steps, the quality and complexity of the generated problems may still vary, potentially impacting the reliability of the benchmark. Manually verifying the quality of some samples from AutoCodeBench by experts is very crucial.
*   **Multilingual Coverage Imbalance:** It is unclear if the problems are equally complex and representative across all 20 languages. Some languages may be under-represented in terms of difficulty and problem types. Furthermore, their approximate language translation may lead to lower quality prompts.
*   **Limited Analysis of Failure Cases:** The paper focuses on overall performance metrics. A more in-depth analysis of specific failure cases would provide valuable insights into the limitations of current LLMs.

**Significance:**

AutoCodeBench has the potential to become a widely adopted benchmark for evaluating code generation capabilities of LLMs, especially in multilingual settings. It also provides the community with a tool for generating training data. The limitations regarding bias and data quality need to be addressed in future work to increase the benchmark's reliability and impact. Furthermore, an interesting extension would be to adapt the benchmark to evaluate other tasks such as code review, debugging or refactoring.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a significant advancement in code generation benchmarking. While there are valid concerns about LLM bias and limitations in dataset quality, the automation and multilingual coverage contribute to a positive influence on the field.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### SMA: Who Said That? Auditing Membership Leakage in Semi-Black-box RAG Controlling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.09105v1)
- **Authors**: Shixuan Sun, Siyuan Liang, Ruoyu Chen, Jianjie Huang, Jingzhi Li, Xiaochun Cao
- **Abstract**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) and its Multimodal Retrieval-Augmented Generation (MRAG) significantly improve the knowledge coverage and contextual understanding of Large Language Models (LLMs) by introducing external knowledge sources. However, retrieval and multimodal fusion obscure content provenance, rendering existing membership inference methods unable to reliably attribute generated outputs to pre-training, external retrieval, or user input, thus undermining privacy leakage accountability To address these challenges, we propose the first Source-aware Membership Audit (SMA) that enables fine-grained source attribution of generated content in a semi-black-box setting with retrieval control capabilities.To address the environmental constraints of semi-black-box auditing, we further design an attribution estimation mechanism based on zero-order optimization, which robustly approximates the true influence of input tokens on the output through large-scale perturbation sampling and ridge regression modeling. In addition, SMA introduces a cross-modal attribution technique that projects image inputs into textual descriptions via MLLMs, enabling token-level attribution in the text modality, which for the first time facilitates membership inference on image retrieval traces in MRAG systems. This work shifts the focus of membership inference from 'whether the data has been memorized' to 'where the content is sourced from', offering a novel perspective for auditing data provenance in complex generative systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Source-aware Membership Audit (SMA), a novel framework designed to audit membership leakage in Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) and Multimodal RAG (MRAG) systems. Unlike traditional Membership Inference Attacks (MIAs) that focus on whether data has been memorized, SMA aims to determine the *source* of leaked content: the model's pre-training corpus or external retrieval results. SMA operates in a semi-black-box setting, utilizing input perturbations and a zero-gradient scoring mechanism to estimate the influence of input tokens on the output. It also includes a cross-modal attribution technique for MRAG that projects image inputs into textual descriptions, enabling token-level attribution. The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of SMA on various textual and multimodal RAG benchmarks, showing improvements over state-of-the-art MIA baselines in terms of accuracy and coverage.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in shifting the focus of membership inference from simple memorization detection to source attribution within RAG and MRAG systems. This is a significant advancement, as it addresses the limitations of existing MIAs in the context of dynamic knowledge integration.  The introduction of a zero-gradient attribution mechanism for semi-black-box settings is also a valuable contribution, especially considering the increasing prevalence of closed-source LLM APIs. Finally, the cross-modal attribution technique for MRAG is a genuinely novel aspect that hasn't been tackled adequately in prior work.

* **Significance:**  The work is significant because it addresses a crucial privacy and accountability gap in RAG and MRAG systems. By enabling content traceability, SMA can help identify and mitigate risks associated with accidental content disclosure from external sources or the model's pre-training data. This capability is particularly important for applications dealing with sensitive or proprietary information, where data provenance is paramount.  The ability to perform these audits in a semi-black-box setting makes the framework practically relevant.

* **Strengths:**
    *   **Problem Formulation:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing MIAs in RAG/MRAG and defines a more nuanced and relevant source-aware auditing problem.
    *   **Technical Approach:** The proposed SMA framework combines several innovative techniques, including input perturbation, zero-gradient attribution, and cross-modal attribution, to achieve its goal.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The authors provide extensive experimental results on multiple benchmarks and models, demonstrating the effectiveness of SMA and comparing it against state-of-the-art baselines.  The ablation studies further highlight the contribution of different components of the framework.
    *   **Practical Relevance:** The semi-black-box nature of SMA makes it applicable to real-world RAG/MRAG deployments, where full access to model internals is often unavailable.

* **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational Cost:** The reliance on input perturbations can be computationally expensive, especially for large models and complex RAG/MRAG systems.  While the authors acknowledge this limitation, further exploration of optimization techniques would be beneficial. The precise costing equations are helpful but more analysis on reducing this cost should be presented.
    *   **Parameter Sensitivity:** The framework's performance can be sensitive to the choice of hyperparameters, such as the number of perturbations and the similarity weights. While the paper discusses parameter sensitivity, more guidance on selecting optimal parameter values would be valuable.
    *   **Limited to LLM's Maximum Token Limit & Sampling Temperature:** As the paper mentions, the framework is limited by the LLM model's maximum token limit and also sampling temperature. With the technology being increasingly commoditized, this is an important limitation.
    *   **Assumption of retrieval toggle:** The requirement that the retrieval module can be toggled on/off is a limitation; some platforms may not offer this functionality.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of privacy and security in generative AI.  It provides a valuable tool for auditing RAG/MRAG systems and ensuring responsible use of these technologies. The source-aware membership inference framework could inspire further research on content traceability and provenance in complex generative systems.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of privacy and security in generative AI. The idea of source-aware membership inference is original and addresses a critical gap in existing MIA techniques for RAG and MRAG systems. The technical approach is well-designed and empirically validated. While the framework has some limitations, its strengths outweigh its weaknesses, making it a valuable contribution to the field with potential for significant impact. The limitations regarding computational cost and parameter sensitivity prevents this from being a 9. The reliance on LLM maximum token limit also prevents a higher score. However, the semi-black-box approach is a positive feature.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 8/10

### SinLlama -- A Large Language Model for Sinhala
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.09115v1)
- **Authors**: H. W. K. Aravinda, Rashad Sirajudeen, Samith Karunathilake, Nisansa de Silva, Surangika Ranathunga, Rishemjit Kaur
- **Abstract**: Low-resource languages such as Sinhala are often overlooked by open-source Large Language Models (LLMs). In this research, we extend an existing multilingual LLM (Llama-3-8B) to better serve Sinhala. We enhance the LLM tokenizer with Sinhala specific vocabulary and perform continual pre-training on a cleaned 10 million Sinhala corpus, resulting in the SinLlama model. This is the very first decoder-based open-source LLM with explicit Sinhala support. When SinLlama was instruction fine-tuned for three text classification tasks, it outperformed base and instruct variants of Llama-3-8B by a significant margin.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "SinLlama," a Large Language Model (LLM) specifically adapted for the Sinhala language. The authors started with the open-source Llama-3-8B model and enhanced it by: 1) extending the tokenizer with Sinhala-specific vocabulary and 2) conducting continual pre-training using a cleaned Sinhala corpus of 10 million sentences. The resulting SinLlama model is positioned as the first open-source, decoder-based LLM with explicit Sinhala support. The authors evaluate SinLlama on three Sinhala text classification tasks (writing style, news categorization, and sentiment analysis), demonstrating that fine-tuned SinLlama outperforms both the base Llama-3-8B and Llama-3-8B-instruct models. The paper promises to release the model publicly upon acceptance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty of the work lies in the adaptation of a state-of-the-art LLM (Llama-3-8B) to a low-resource language, Sinhala, and the explicit creation of a publicly available resource for the language. While the techniques themselves (tokenizer extension and continual pre-training) are well-established in the field, their application to Sinhala in the context of LLMs represents a meaningful contribution. Claims of it being the first open-source decoder-based with Sinhala support will need verifying. The model size is small and the training corpus is also small.

*   **Significance:** Addressing the performance gap of LLMs on low-resource languages is crucial for reducing the digital divide. By creating SinLlama, the authors provide a valuable tool for NLP research and applications in the Sinhala-speaking community.  Outperforming the base Llama models on Sinhala-specific tasks confirms the need for such adaptation. The public release of SinLlama should contribute to future research on Sinhala NLP.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem statement and motivation: The paper effectively highlights the challenges faced by low-resource languages in the era of LLMs.
    *   Straightforward and replicable methodology: The approach is well-defined and uses standard techniques, facilitating replication and extension by other researchers.
    *   Empirical validation: The experiments are relevant and demonstrate the effectiveness of SinLlama compared to strong baselines.
    *   Promise of public release: The open-source nature of SinLlama enhances its potential impact on the field.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited evaluation scope: The evaluation focuses only on text classification tasks. It would be beneficial to assess SinLlama's performance on other Sinhala NLP tasks, such as text generation, machine translation, or question answering.
    *   Modest Dataset Size: A 10 million sentence corpus while a good start is significantly smaller than the trillions of tokens used to train baseline LLMs.
    *   Lack of deeper analysis: While the results show performance improvements, the paper lacks a detailed analysis of why SinLlama performs better. It would be interesting to examine the impact of the vocabulary extension or the types of linguistic knowledge learned during continual pre-training.
    *   Incremental Contribution: The paper uses already known techniques and adapts them to SinLlama which limits its contribution to the field.
    *   Model Size and Resource Requirements: Llama-3-8B is not a large model.

*   **Potential Influence:** SinLlama has the potential to:
    *   Spur further research on Sinhala NLP, leveraging LLMs.
    *   Facilitate the development of Sinhala language applications.
    *   Serve as a case study for adapting LLMs to other low-resource languages.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a valuable, albeit incremental, contribution to the field of LLMs and low-resource language processing. By creating SinLlama, the authors offer a tangible resource for the Sinhala-speaking community and a starting point for future research. While the novelty is not groundbreaking and the analysis could be more in-depth, the practical impact of SinLlama warrants recognition.

**Score: 6**

**Rationale:** The score reflects the combination of the paper's clear value in providing a practical resource for Sinhala NLP with the limitations in terms of novelty and depth of analysis. The adaptation of existing techniques to a specific low-resource language is important, but the paper lacks the theoretical insights or methodological innovations that would justify a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### OpenCUA: Open Foundations for Computer-Use Agents
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.09123v1)
- **Authors**: Xinyuan Wang, Bowen Wang, Dunjie Lu, Junlin Yang, Tianbao Xie, Junli Wang, Jiaqi Deng, Xiaole Guo, Yiheng Xu, Chen Henry Wu, Zhennan Shen, Zhuokai Li, Ryan Li, Xiaochuan Li, Junda Chen, Boyuan Zheng, Peihang Li, Fangyu Lei, Ruisheng Cao, Yeqiao Fu, Dongchan Shin, Martin Shin, Jiarui Hu, Yuyan Wang, Jixuan Chen, Yuxiao Ye, Danyang Zhang, Dikang Du, Hao Hu, Huarong Chen, Zaida Zhou, Yipu Wang, Heng Wang, Diyi Yang, Victor Zhong, Flood Sung, Y. Charles, Zhilin Yang, Tao Yu
- **Abstract**: Vision-language models have demonstrated impressive capabilities as computer-use agents (CUAs) capable of automating diverse computer tasks. As their commercial potential grows, critical details of the most capable CUA systems remain closed. As these agents will increasingly mediate digital interactions and execute consequential decisions on our behalf, the research community needs access to open CUA frameworks to study their capabilities, limitations, and risks. To bridge this gap, we propose OpenCUA, a comprehensive open-source framework for scaling CUA data and foundation models. Our framework consists of: (1) an annotation infrastructure that seamlessly captures human computer-use demonstrations; (2) AgentNet, the first large-scale computer-use task dataset spanning 3 operating systems and 200+ applications and websites; (3) a scalable pipeline that transforms demonstrations into state-action pairs with reflective long Chain-of-Thought reasoning that sustain robust performance gains as data scales. Our end-to-end agent models demonstrate strong performance across CUA benchmarks. In particular, OpenCUA-32B achieves an average success rate of 34.8% on OSWorld-Verified, establishing a new state-of-the-art (SOTA) among open-source models and surpassing OpenAI CUA (GPT-4o). Further analysis confirms that our approach generalizes well across domains and benefits significantly from increased test-time computation. We release our annotation tool, datasets, code, and models to build open foundations for further CUA research.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary**

The paper introduces OPENCUA, an open-source framework designed to facilitate research in computer-use agents (CUAs).  The framework consists of an annotation tool for collecting human computer-use demonstrations, a large-scale dataset called AGENTNET spanning multiple operating systems and applications, and a scalable pipeline for converting demonstrations into state-action pairs with Chain-of-Thought reasoning.  The authors train end-to-end agent models using this framework and demonstrate strong performance on CUA benchmarks.  Notably, their OPENCUA-32B model achieves a new state-of-the-art among open-source models on the OSWorld-Verified benchmark, even surpassing proprietary models like OpenAI's CUA (GPT-4o). The paper emphasizes the release of all components (tool, data, code, and models) to promote open CUA research.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates reasonable novelty. While the concept of computer-use agents is not new, the creation and release of a comprehensive, fully open-source framework like OPENCUA, including a large-scale dataset, annotation tool, and data processing pipeline, represents a significant contribution. The reflective CoT synthesis method is also a valuable methodological contribution. However, the underlying models are built on existing open-source VLMs, which reduces the novelty score somewhat.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work lies in its potential to democratize CUA research. The closed nature of leading CUA systems has been a barrier to progress, and OPENCUA addresses this directly by providing the research community with the resources necessary to study, reproduce, and extend CUA capabilities.  The performance achieved by OPENCUA-32B, exceeding open-source baselines and rivaling proprietary systems, further underscores the potential of this framework. Furthermore, the paper includes a detailed error study, which is critical for improving agent robustness. The work contributes towards making the development of CUA's more accessible and understandable. The framework is also validated with detailed analysis of performance vs data size and other parameters.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive and Open:** The most significant strength is the release of the entire framework, encouraging reproducibility and further research.
    *   **Large-Scale Dataset:** The AGENTNET dataset is a valuable resource, capturing diverse computer-use scenarios.
    *   **Strong Performance:** The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach.
    *   **Detailed Analysis:** The paper includes thorough analyses of model structure, data scaling, error modes and cross-domain generalization.
    *   **Reflection Component for Reasoning:** The innovation of a CoT that allows agents to not only detect prior errors, but also correct for it helps provide more agent capability.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Existing Models:** The core agent models are based on existing VLMs like Qwen, limiting architectural innovation.
    *   **Complexity of Deployment:** While the framework is open-source, setting up and running the entire pipeline might still present a barrier for some researchers with limited resources. Online metrics for evaluation require considerable computational resources making online testing not always possible.
    *   **Online Evaluation Requirements:** To perform an evaluation from online metrics, the OSWorld environment requires a considerable amount of runtime resources and setup effort, impacting efficiency.

*   **Potential Influence:** OPENCUA has the potential to become a widely adopted framework for CUA research, fostering innovation and collaboration in the field. It could influence future research directions by providing a common platform for evaluating new methods and models. The comprehensive nature of the OPENCUA framework will enable the research community to systematically investigate these agents' capabilities, limitations, and risks as they increasingly mediate our digital interactions.

*Rigorous Rationale for Score:*
This paper is important in bringing open research to the field of CUAs. The framework enables researchers to participate in this domain in a way that hasn't been previously available. However, there are components that are somewhat limited - the model itself relies on previous work, and deployment can be cumbersome. On the whole, while it is not the theoretical breakthrough that some seek, it represents an important practical and technical contribution that has considerable value.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI

### OdysseyBench: Evaluating LLM Agents on Long-Horizon Complex Office Application Workflows
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.09124v1)
- **Authors**: Weixuan Wang, Dongge Han, Daniel Madrigal Diaz, Jin Xu, Victor Rühle, Saravan Rajmohan
- **Abstract**: Autonomous agents powered by large language models (LLMs) are increasingly deployed in real-world applications requiring complex, long-horizon workflows. However, existing benchmarks predominantly focus on atomic tasks that are self-contained and independent, failing to capture the long-term contextual dependencies and multi-interaction coordination required in realistic scenarios. To address this gap, we introduce OdysseyBench, a comprehensive benchmark for evaluating LLM agents on long-horizon workflows across diverse office applications including Word, Excel, PDF, Email, and Calendar. Our benchmark comprises two complementary splits: OdysseyBench+ with 300 tasks derived from real-world use cases, and OdysseyBench-Neo with 302 newly synthesized complex tasks. Each task requires agent to identify essential information from long-horizon interaction histories and perform multi-step reasoning across various applications. To enable scalable benchmark creation, we propose HomerAgents, a multi-agent framework that automates the generation of long-horizon workflow benchmarks through systematic environment exploration, task generation, and dialogue synthesis. Our extensive evaluation demonstrates that OdysseyBench effectively challenges state-of-the-art LLM agents, providing more accurate assessment of their capabilities in complex, real-world contexts compared to existing atomic task benchmarks. We believe that OdysseyBench will serve as a valuable resource for advancing the development and evaluation of LLM agents in real-world productivity scenarios. In addition, we release OdysseyBench and HomerAgents to foster research along this line.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "ODYSSEYBENCH: EVALUATING LLM AGENTS ON LONG-HORIZON COMPLEX OFFICE APPLICATION WORKFLOWS":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces OdysseyBench, a new benchmark designed to evaluate the performance of Large Language Model (LLM) agents in complex, long-horizon office application workflows. Recognizing that existing benchmarks often focus on isolated, atomic tasks, OdysseyBench aims to capture the complexities of real-world scenarios where agents need to maintain context, reason across multiple steps, and coordinate actions across various applications (Word, Excel, PDF, Email, Calendar).

The benchmark consists of two splits: OdysseyBench+, derived from real-world use cases, and OdysseyBench-Neo, which features newly synthesized complex tasks.  To facilitate scalable benchmark creation, the authors propose HOMERAGENTS, a multi-agent framework that automates the generation of long-horizon workflow benchmarks through environment exploration, task generation, and dialogue synthesis.  The paper presents an evaluation of state-of-the-art LLM agents on OdysseyBench, highlighting the limitations of current models in these complex scenarios compared to their performance on atomic task benchmarks. The authors release both the OdysseyBench and HOMERAGENTS framework.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the **benchmark itself**. Existing agent benchmarks, as the authors rightly point out, tend to be atomized. OdysseyBench directly addresses the need for evaluating *long-horizon, contextual reasoning* in office environments. The creation of the OdysseyBench-Neo split, with newly synthesized complex tasks, is also a valuable contribution. However, similar efforts to create more realistic, multi-step task environments for LLM evaluation have emerged recently. The technical novelty of HOMERAGENTS as a generation framework is moderate. Multi-agent systems for data generation are not entirely new, but its specific application to office workflow simulation, and the design of its components, do introduce a valuable methodology.

*   **Significance:**  The paper addresses a very important and pressing need in the field: better evaluation of LLM agents in real-world applications.  The current trend of deploying LLMs in complex environments *necessitates* more rigorous evaluation beyond simple task completion rates. OdysseyBench has the potential to become a valuable resource for the community, driving research towards agents capable of handling the complexities of office workflows.  The release of the dataset and code will further facilitate adoption and extension of the benchmark. However, it's important to acknowledge that the scope is somewhat limited to office-related tasks. The framework might not be directly applicable to other domains without substantial modifications. The multi-agent framework may need further maturation and be accessible more easily to the community to make significant widespread impact.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Focus on a relevant and challenging problem: evaluating long-horizon reasoning in LLM agents.
    *   The creation of a new benchmark (OdysseyBench) that addresses the limitations of existing benchmarks.
    *   The automated generation framework (HOMERAGENTS) enabling scalable benchmark creation.
    *   Comprehensive evaluation of state-of-the-art LLM agents, providing valuable insights into their strengths and weaknesses.
    *   Dataset and code release for community benefit.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The novelty of HOMERAGENTS generation framework is incremental.
    *   The benchmark is limited to office application tasks, potentially limiting its broader applicability.
    *   The tasks, while more complex, may still not fully capture the unpredictable and nuanced nature of real-world human-agent interactions. The evaluation is still primarily pass/fail, more fine-grained metrics could be useful for further assessing model capabilities.
    *   While the human evaluation offers some validation of task coherence and solvability, it is performed on only a sampled subset of the tasks.

*   **Potential Influence:**  If the benchmark is adopted by the community, it could significantly influence the direction of research in LLM agent development, pushing the field towards more realistic and practical evaluation scenarios. The findings from the paper could guide the development of new architectures and training methods that are better suited for handling complex, long-horizon tasks.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of LLM agent evaluation. The creation of the OdysseyBench benchmark, with its focus on long-horizon reasoning and multi-application workflows, is particularly significant. While the novelty of the HOMERAGENTS framework is moderate, the framework does offer a valuable methodology. OdysseyBench also addresses a crucial gap in current evaluation methodologies.

Therefore, considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, a score of **7** is appropriate.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Complex Logical Instruction Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.09125v1)
- **Authors**: Mian Zhang, Shujian Liu, Sixun Dong, Ming Yin, Yebowen Hu, Xun Wang, Steven Ma, Song Wang, Sathish Reddy Indurthi, Haoyun Deng, Zhiyu Zoey Chen, Kaiqiang Song
- **Abstract**: Instruction following has catalyzed the recent era of Large Language Models (LLMs) and is the foundational skill underpinning more advanced capabilities such as reasoning and agentic behaviors. As tasks grow more challenging, the logic structures embedded in natural language instructions becomes increasingly intricate. However, how well LLMs perform on such logic-rich instructions remains under-explored. We propose LogicIFGen and LogicIFEval. LogicIFGen is a scalable, automated framework for generating verifiable instructions from code functions, which can naturally express rich logic such as conditionals, nesting, recursion, and function calls. We further curate a collection of complex code functions and use LogicIFGen to construct LogicIFEval, a benchmark comprising 426 verifiable logic-rich instructions. Our experiments demonstrate that current state-of-the-art LLMs still struggle to correctly follow the instructions in LogicIFEval. Most LLMs can only follow fewer than 60% of the instructions, revealing significant deficiencies in the instruction-following ability. Code and Benchmark: https://github.com/mianzhang/LogicIF
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Complex Logical Instruction Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces `LogicIFGen`, a framework for automatically generating verifiable instructions from code functions with complex logic (conditionals, nesting, recursion, function calls). It also presents `LogicIFEval`, a benchmark based on 426 instructions generated using `LogicIFGen`. Experiments using `LogicIFEval` show that state-of-the-art LLMs struggle with these logic-rich instructions, with many models achieving less than 60% accuracy. The paper also includes an error analysis and shows that larger LLMs benefit from incorporating explicit thinking during the execution process.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's main novelty lies in its **automated framework for generating instruction-following tasks from code functions expressing complex logic**. While instruction following and code generation benchmarks exist, `LogicIFGen` provides a scalable way to create verifiable tasks with varied logical structures. This approach differs from manually curated datasets, which can be labor-intensive and limited in their ability to represent different logical patterns.

*   **Significance:**  The findings reveal a **significant gap in LLMs' ability to follow logic-rich natural language instructions**, even when not requiring code generation. This underscores the importance of evaluating instruction-following beyond simple content constraints or format requirements, and highlight a potential area for future research to enhance model reasoning capabilities. The benchmark `LogicIFEval` itself serves as a valuable tool for assessing LLMs on this challenging task and driving progress in this area. The framework's capability to monitor intermediate steps is a key aspect for diagnostic purposes, and also makes the framework well suited for future studies.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Scalable Generation:** `LogicIFGen` enables the generation of verifiable instruction-following examples in a scalable manner, leveraging code functions.
    *   **Focus on Complex Logic:** The benchmark fills a gap by specifically targeting instructions with intricate logical structures (nested conditions, recursion etc.).
    *   **Verifiability:** Instructions are generated from code, allowing for precise and automated evaluation based on code execution. This is an advantage over relying on potentially subjective human evaluation or LLM-based judgment.
    *   **Error Analysis:** The paper provides insights into common failure modes, helping understand where models struggle.
    *   **Intermediate steps captured:** The framework captures intermidiate steps making it a valuable tool for analyzing a models instruction following abilities.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Model Scope:** While the study examines a reasonable set of models, continual advancements in the field suggest that newer architectures might exhibit different capabilities, which warrants continuous updates and re-evaluation of benchmarks.
    *   **Dependency on Code Quality:** `LogicIFGen`'s success depends on the quality of the initial code functions. Poorly written or complex code could lead to overly convoluted instructions.
    *   **Natural Language Generation:** While the study addresses this, reliance on LLMs for the generation of natural language instructions may inadvertently introduce biases or variations in clarity that can potentially influence model outcomes.
    *   **Complexity Metric**: Although the chosen complexity metric correlates with LLM performance, more advanced or learned weighting schemes could improve its accuracy.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper can influence the field by:

    *   Encouraging more research on improving LLMs' ability to handle complex logic in instruction following.
    *   Providing a valuable benchmark for assessing these capabilities.
    *   Inspiring the development of new techniques for generating challenging and verifiable instruction-following tasks.
    *   Highlighting the importance of explicit reasoning mechanisms in LLMs.

*   **Justification for Score:**

The paper offers a clear and well-executed contribution, with a novel approach to automatic instruction generation and a valuable benchmark that addresses a critical gap in current LLM evaluations. The experiments and analyses provide solid evidence for their claims. However, the limitations related to the chosen models and the scope of the complexity metric and natural language generation must be considered.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Training-Free Text-Guided Color Editing with Multi-Modal Diffusion Transformer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.09131v1)
- **Authors**: Zixin Yin, Xili Dai, Ling-Hao Chen, Deyu Zhou, Jianan Wang, Duomin Wang, Gang Yu, Lionel M. Ni, Heung-Yeung Shum
- **Abstract**: Text-guided color editing in images and videos is a fundamental yet unsolved problem, requiring fine-grained manipulation of color attributes, including albedo, light source color, and ambient lighting, while preserving physical consistency in geometry, material properties, and light-matter interactions. Existing training-free methods offer broad applicability across editing tasks but struggle with precise color control and often introduce visual inconsistency in both edited and non-edited regions. In this work, we present ColorCtrl, a training-free color editing method that leverages the attention mechanisms of modern Multi-Modal Diffusion Transformers (MM-DiT). By disentangling structure and color through targeted manipulation of attention maps and value tokens, our method enables accurate and consistent color editing, along with word-level control of attribute intensity. Our method modifies only the intended regions specified by the prompt, leaving unrelated areas untouched. Extensive experiments on both SD3 and FLUX.1-dev demonstrate that ColorCtrl outperforms existing training-free approaches and achieves state-of-the-art performances in both edit quality and consistency. Furthermore, our method surpasses strong commercial models such as FLUX.1 Kontext Max and GPT-4o Image Generation in terms of consistency. When extended to video models like CogVideoX, our approach exhibits greater advantages, particularly in maintaining temporal coherence and editing stability. Finally, our method also generalizes to instruction-based editing diffusion models such as Step1X-Edit and FLUX.1 Kontext dev, further demonstrating its versatility.
- **Summary**: Here is a concise summary and a critical evaluation of the research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ColorCtrl, a novel training-free method for text-guided color editing of images and videos. It leverages the attention mechanisms within Multi-Modal Diffusion Transformers (MM-DiT) to achieve accurate and consistent color changes while preserving other visual attributes like geometry, material properties, and light-matter interactions. The method allows for word-level control of color attribute intensity and generalizes well across different MM-DiT-based models, including video and instruction-based editing models. Experimental results demonstrate ColorCtrl's superior performance compared to existing training-free approaches and even some commercial models in terms of edit quality, consistency, and realism.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of text-guided image and video editing. The key strength lies in its ability to achieve fine-grained color manipulation without requiring retraining, offering a significant advantage over methods that require extensive datasets and complex training pipelines. The technique effectively disentangles structure and color through attention map manipulation, enabling precise and consistent edits across various visual elements, including reflections and refractions.

*Novelty*: The core idea of leveraging the attention mechanisms of MM-DiTs for training-free color editing is novel. The specific techniques for preserving geometry, material properties, and controlling attribute intensity through attention map manipulation demonstrate a good level of technical innovation.  The method's ability to generalize across different MM-DiT models (image, video, instruction-based editing) further enhances its novelty and practical value. The color preservation mask using the vision-to-text attention part is a nice technical trick.

*Significance*: The ability to perform high-quality, controllable color editing without retraining has significant implications for various applications, including film production, content creation, and visual design. The superior performance compared to existing training-free methods and commercial models suggests the potential for widespread adoption. The ability to generalize the method to diverse architectures further expands its influence.

*Weaknesses*:  A major limitation is the reliance on the underlying generative model's capabilities. As acknowledged by the authors, failures in object detection or attribute understanding by the foundation model directly affect the quality of edits.  While the method performs well in many cases, a more thorough exploration of failure modes and limitations would further strengthen the analysis. The color results are good, but there is something slightly 'off' with the illumination of the resulting edits, which could be further improved.

*Justification for Score*: The paper demonstrates significant technical innovation in a relevant field with valuable application, and the performance evaluation supports its claim, but limitations with the underlying models and subtle lighting artifacts warrant a non-exceptional rating.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.GR
